<position-changes>
ACCEPTED, all of it. Every FIX in your voided round 2 reply has been
applied. Nothing was refuted and nothing was struck.

Your A-section and both sweeps produced ten residual items. In order:

1. The record cap invented malformed records. Truncation is now the
   reader's own state, reported as "the remainder was NOT examined".
2. The cap applied after the split. A new `Read-BoundedRecords` uses a
   `StringReader` and stops AT the cap.
3. Empty records were dropped as "trailing artifacts". `ReadLine`
   returns nothing for a file ending in a newline, so the artifact does
   not exist; the condition was removed rather than refined, and every
   empty line now counts as malformed.
4. The root helper reported resolution failures as roots. It returns
   `ok`, `root` or `error`, and both callers report the difference.
5. The extra-input guard compared spelling. An extra input reached
   through a directory link is now refused outright.
6. The extra-input fragment used variables the resolver does not define.
   The plan carries the real loop over `$ExtraInputPaths`.
7. The root test had the wrong oracle and never reached the helper.
   There are now direct unit tests that extract the function from the
   shipped file, plus an integration test whose assertion matches what
   actually fires.
8. The renderer interface promised "every separator character". It now
   names the five categories and says `SpaceSeparator` is not one.
9. Two paragraphs still said "every path". Both carry the deletion-only
   qualification.
10. Both test selectors matched substrings and missed cases. Both name
    every test and state the expected collected count.

Your section B accepted both wording-only dispositions. They stand
unchanged.
</position-changes>

docs/superpowers/plans/rounds/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window/README.md:196:    records with it. `ReadLine` returns nothing for a file ending in a
docs/superpowers/plans/rounds/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window/README.md:197:    newline, so the artifact does not exist and every empty line is now
docs/superpowers/plans/rounds/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window/README.md:198:    counted as malformed. The condition was removed rather than refined.
docs/superpowers/plans/rounds/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window/README.md:199: 4. **The root helper called resolution failures roots.** Both catch

docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:1: # Mirror Identity Window Implementation Plan
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:2: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:3: > **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:4: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:5: **Goal:** Make the review mirror's identity refusal name the paths that moved, and ship the quiet-period rule that the refusal enforces, so a session outside this repo can act on both.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:6: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:7: **Architecture:** Three independent changes and no change to the gate. The build writes the source side's content manifest to a sibling of the mirror directory, under the same destination guards `-OverrideOut` already carries; the source-status refusal reads that sibling, on that path only, to name what entered coverage, left coverage, or changed content; and the skill grows a contract region plus a timing rule stating that nothing may write inside the reviewed repository between the build and the wrapper's exit. The sidecar file is advisory: it is read after the comparison has already decided to block, so no state it can be in changes any exit code.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:8: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:9: **Tech Stack:** Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7 (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1`), Python 3 with pytest (`evals/multi-model-verify/`), Markdown contract regions under `skills/`.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:10: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:11: **Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window-design.md`
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:12: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:13: ## Global Constraints
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:14: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:15: - `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1` is **Windows PowerShell 5.1 compatible and ASCII ONLY**. No smart quotes, no dashes outside the ASCII hyphen, no non-ASCII byte anywhere in the file.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:16: - **No VERIFICATION verdict may change.** Every existing `-VerifyIdentity` outcome stays exactly what it is today, and the new explanation only ADDS printed output on a path already exiting 1. This is NOT a blanket ban on new exit codes: Task 1 deliberately adds CONSTRUCTION refusals that exit 2 for destinations the build previously accepted unguarded. Adding a build refusal is in scope; changing what a verify decides is not.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:17: - **Tests first.** Write the failing test, run it, watch it fail for the stated reason, then implement.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:18: - **Do not bump `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` in this plan.** The bump happens after the diff debate, per `CLAUDE.md`.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:19: - **Do not run pytest, the gates, or any repo-writing command while a review round is in flight.** That is the defect this plan documents.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:20: - A new contract region needs BOTH a whole-region pin in `evals/multi-model-verify/` AND an entry in `DECLARED_REGIONS` in `evals/multi-model-verify/test_contract_coverage.py`.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:21: - A pin asserted with `in text` against the raw file matches **line breaks and leading indentation exactly**. Write the Markdown first, then copy those lines byte for byte into the pin.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:22: - Commits are lowercase imperative with no AI attribution. **A commit message must not contain a bare `-Word` token**: the family git guard reads it as a passed flag and denies the commit (backlog item 79).
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:23: - Work on a feature branch, never on `main`.

docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:27: ### Task 1: The build writes the source manifest beside the mirror
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:28: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:29: **Files:**
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:30: - Modify: `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1` (the `Get-StatusSha256` return at `:662-694`, two new functions beside it, the guard block at `:951-1008`, the alias guards at `:1258-1290`, and the record block at `:1737-1756`)
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:31: - Test: `evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py`
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:32: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:33: **Interfaces:**
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:34: - Consumes: nothing from earlier tasks.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:35: - Produces:
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:36:   - `Get-StatusSha256($repo)` returns `@{ Ok = $true; Sha = <hex>; Manifest = <string[]> }` on success, unchanged `@{ Ok = $false; Reason = <string> }` on failure. `Manifest` holds `Get-ContentManifest`'s lines, each `"<relpath> <sha256hex>"`.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:37:   - `Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath($mirrorPath)` returns `"<full mirror path>.source-manifest"`, or `$null` when the mirror path is a filesystem root and therefore has no sibling.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:38:   - `Write-SourceManifestSidecar($path, $manifestLines)` returns `$true` or `$false`. It opens the file with `CreateNew`, which makes the FINAL PATH COMPONENT safe against an overwrite and against a link substituted there. It does not and cannot defend a DIRECTORY component; that is the alias guards' job, in Step 5b.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:39:   - The build's record block gains one line, `source_manifest: <path>`, printed after `override:`.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:40: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:41: **Why this task is mostly guards.** The sidecar path is DERIVED, not supplied, and a derived string written without a guard is a write into whatever happens to sit there. A link at that path carries the write through to its target, and a target inside the repository would be corrupted by the very function that exists to explain a corrupted repository.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:42: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:43: Cross-vendor review found that the first draft had NO destination guards, and then found that the second draft's claim to carry "the override's whole guard set" was false. The override's protection is in TWO places: the lexical block at `:951-1008`, and the alias block at `:1258-1290` that walks each path's ancestors for a reparse point and checks it against every followed link target. The second draft copied only the first. This task copies both, and it validates completely BEFORE it removes anything.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:44: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:45: - [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:48:

docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:53:     A SIBLING of the mirror, never a child and never inside the repo:
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:54:     a child would enter mirror_state_sha256 and a file in the repo would
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:55:     enter source_status_sha256, and this one must enter neither.

docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:162: def extract_ps_function(name):
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:163:     """Pull one function's text out of the SHIPPED script so a unit test
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:164:     exercises the same source the build runs. Copying the body into the
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:165:     test would create a second definition that drifts, which is the
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:166:     failure this repo keeps finding in its own records."""
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:167:     text = MIRROR.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:168:     start = text.index("function " + name + "(")
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:169:     k = text.index("{", start)
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:170:     depth = 0
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:171:     while True:
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:172:         if text[k] == "{":
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:173:             depth += 1
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:174:         elif text[k] == "}":
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:175:             depth -= 1
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:176:             if depth == 0:
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:177:                 break
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:178:         k += 1
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:179:     return text[start:k + 1]
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:180: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:181: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:182: @pytest.mark.parametrize("path,kind", [
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:183:     ("C:\\", "root"),
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:184:     ("\\\\server\\share\\", "root"),
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:185:     ("\\\\server\\share\\dir", "ok"),
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:186:     ("C:\\Temp\\mirror", "ok"),
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:187: ])
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:188: def test_the_sidecar_helper_classifies_roots_directly(tmp_path, path, kind):
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:189:     """The helper's own contract, exercised directly.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:190: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:191:     The integration path CANNOT establish this: the mirror-path guards
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:192:     refuse a root long before the sidecar is derived, so a build-level
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:193:     assertion never reaches the helper. Measured 2026-09-05 on
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:194:     PowerShell 7, a root mirror path on a repo living on C: is refused
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:195:     by the containment guard with "the mirror path contains the repo",
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:196:     which says nothing about roots at all.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:197:     """
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:198:     src = extract_ps_function("Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath")
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:199:     driver = tmp_path / "probe.ps1"
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:200:     driver.write_text(
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:201:         src + "\n$r = Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath $args[0]\n"
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:202:         "Write-Output $r.Kind\n", encoding="utf-8")
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:203:     proc = subprocess.run(
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:204:         [ps_host(), "-NoProfile", "-NonInteractive", "-File", str(driver),
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:205:          path],
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:206:         capture_output=True, text=True)
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:207:     assert proc.returncode == 0, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:208:     assert proc.stdout.strip() == kind, (path, proc.stdout)
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:209: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:210: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:211: def test_a_resolution_failure_is_not_reported_as_a_root(tmp_path):
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:212:     """Two outcomes that must not share a message. Windows PowerShell
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:213:     5.1 refused a 278-character absolute path from GetFullPath with
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:214:     PathTooLongException while PowerShell 7 accepted it, so on 5.1 a
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:215:     long-path build would announce a filesystem root and never reach the
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:216:     path-budget refusal that names the real problem."""
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:217:     src = extract_ps_function("Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath")
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:218:     driver = tmp_path / "probe.ps1"
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:219:     driver.write_text(
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:220:         src + "\n$r = Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath $args[0]\n"
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:221:         "Write-Output $r.Kind\n", encoding="utf-8")
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:222:     long_path = "C:\\" + ("d" * 200 + "\\") * 2 + "leaf"
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:223:     proc = subprocess.run(

docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:225:          long_path],
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:226:         capture_output=True, text=True)
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:227:     assert proc.returncode == 0, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:228:     assert proc.stdout.strip() in ("ok", "error"), proc.stdout
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:229:     assert proc.stdout.strip() != "root", "a resolution failure read as a root"
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:230: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:231: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:232: def test_a_root_mirror_path_is_refused_by_the_build(tmp_path):
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:233:     """The integration half, with an oracle that matches what actually
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:234:     fires. Which guard refuses depends on where the repo lives, so this
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:235:     asserts only that the build refuses; the helper's root contract is
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:236:     the unit test above."""
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:237:     repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:238:     proc = run_mirror(repo, pathlib.Path("C:\\"))
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:239:     assert proc.returncode == 2, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:240:     assert "ERROR:" in proc.stdout, proc.stdout
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:241: ```
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:242: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:243: Add `import pathlib` to the module's imports if it is not already there. `subprocess` and `pytest` are already imported.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:244: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:245: **Why a unit test and not just an integration one.** The mirror-path guards refuse a root long before the sidecar is derived, so no build-level assertion can reach the helper, and an earlier draft's integration assertion on the word `root` failed against the containment guard's actual message. `extract_ps_function` reads the function out of the shipped file, so the unit test and the build share one definition.
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:246: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:247: - [ ] **Step 2: Run the tests and confirm they fail for the stated reason**
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:248: 
docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:249: Run, naming every test rather than matching on substrings, because a selector that quietly matches fewer tests than were written is how a suite reports green on cases it never ran:

162: def extract_ps_function(name):
163:     """Pull one function's text out of the SHIPPED script so a unit test
164:     exercises the same source the build runs. Copying the body into the
165:     test would create a second definition that drifts, which is the
166:     failure this repo keeps finding in its own records."""
167:     text = MIRROR.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
168:     start = text.index("function " + name + "(")
169:     k = text.index("{", start)
170:     depth = 0
171:     while True:
172:         if text[k] == "{":
173:             depth += 1
174:         elif text[k] == "}":
175:             depth -= 1
176:             if depth == 0:
177:                 break
178:         k += 1
179:     return text[start:k + 1]
180: 
181: 
182: @pytest.mark.parametrize("path,kind", [
183:     ("C:\\", "root"),
184:     ("\\\\server\\share\\", "root"),
185:     ("\\\\server\\share\\dir", "ok"),
186:     ("C:\\Temp\\mirror", "ok"),
187: ])
188: def test_the_sidecar_helper_classifies_roots_directly(tmp_path, path, kind):
189:     """The helper's own contract, exercised directly.
190: 
191:     The integration path CANNOT establish this: the mirror-path guards
192:     refuse a root long before the sidecar is derived, so a build-level
193:     assertion never reaches the helper. Measured 2026-09-05 on
194:     PowerShell 7, a root mirror path on a repo living on C: is refused
195:     by the containment guard with "the mirror path contains the repo",
196:     which says nothing about roots at all.
197:     """
198:     src = extract_ps_function("Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath")
199:     driver = tmp_path / "probe.ps1"
200:     driver.write_text(
201:         src + "\n$r = Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath $args[0]\n"
202:         "Write-Output $r.Kind\n", encoding="utf-8")
203:     proc = subprocess.run(
204:         [ps_host(), "-NoProfile", "-NonInteractive", "-File", str(driver),
205:          path],
206:         capture_output=True, text=True)
207:     assert proc.returncode == 0, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
208:     assert proc.stdout.strip() == kind, (path, proc.stdout)
209:

211: def test_a_resolution_failure_is_not_reported_as_a_root(tmp_path):
212:     """Two outcomes that must not share a message. Windows PowerShell
213:     5.1 refused a 278-character absolute path from GetFullPath with
214:     PathTooLongException while PowerShell 7 accepted it, so on 5.1 a
215:     long-path build would announce a filesystem root and never reach the
216:     path-budget refusal that names the real problem."""
217:     src = extract_ps_function("Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath")
218:     driver = tmp_path / "probe.ps1"
219:     driver.write_text(
220:         src + "\n$r = Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath $args[0]\n"
221:         "Write-Output $r.Kind\n", encoding="utf-8")
222:     long_path = "C:\\" + ("d" * 200 + "\\") * 2 + "leaf"
223:     proc = subprocess.run(
224:         [ps_host(), "-NoProfile", "-NonInteractive", "-File", str(driver),
225:          long_path],
226:         capture_output=True, text=True)
227:     assert proc.returncode == 0, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
228:     assert proc.stdout.strip() in ("ok", "error"), proc.stdout
229:     assert proc.stdout.strip() != "root", "a resolution failure read as a root"

233:     """The integration half, with an oracle that matches what actually
234:     fires. Which guard refuses depends on where the repo lives, so this
235:     asserts only that the build refuses; the helper's root contract is

243: Add `import pathlib` to the module's imports if it is not already there. `subprocess` and `pytest` are already imported.
244: 
245: **Why a unit test and not just an integration one.** The mirror-path guards refuse a root long before the sidecar is derived, so no build-level assertion can reach the helper, and an earlier draft's integration assertion on the word `root` failed against the containment guard's actual message. `extract_ps_function` reads the function out of the shipped file, so the unit test and the build share one definition.

269: - [ ] **Step 4: Add the two sidecar functions**
270: 
271: Insert immediately after `Get-StatusSha256`'s closing brace, before `$toplevel = $true`:
272: 
273: ```powershell
274: function Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath($mirrorPath) {
275:     # A SIBLING of the mirror, never a child. A file inside the mirror
276:     # would enter mirror_state_sha256 and a file inside the repository
277:     # would enter source_status_sha256; this one must enter neither,
278:     # because it is written after both are measured and read only after
279:     # a comparison has already decided to refuse.
280:     #
281:     # ONE derivation, shared by the build that writes the file and the
282:     # verify that reads it, so the two sides cannot drift apart.
283:     #
284:     # A ROOT HAS NO SIBLING, and the leaf is NOT how you detect one.
285:     # Measured 2026-09-05: `Split-Path 'C:\' -Leaf` returns `C:\` rather
286:     # than `C:`, so a regex on the leaf never fires for a drive root; and
287:     # `Split-Path '\\server\share\' -Leaf` returns `share` with parent
288:     # `\\server`, which appended would name a DIFFERENT SHARE. The two
289:     # hosts do not agree on the UNC case. So the framework's own root is
290:     # the test, and the suffix is APPENDED to the full path rather than
291:     # rejoined to a parent, which removes the UNC rejoin entirely.
292:     # THREE OUTCOMES, not two. An earlier draft returned $null for a root
293:     # AND for a resolution failure, so both callers announced "filesystem
294:     # root" for either. Measured 2026-09-05: Windows PowerShell 5.1's
295:     # provider accepted a 278-character absolute path that GetFullPath
296:     # refused with PathTooLongException, while PowerShell 7 accepted it,
297:     # so a long-path build on 5.1 would have reported a filesystem root
298:     # and never reached the path-budget refusal that names the real
299:     # problem.
300:     $full = $null
301:     try {
302:         $full = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($mirrorPath)
303:     } catch {
304:         return @{ Kind = "error"; Reason = $_.Exception.Message }
305:     }
306:     $full = $full.TrimEnd("\")
307:     $root = $null
308:     try {
309:         $root = [System.IO.Path]::GetPathRoot($full)
310:     } catch {
311:         return @{ Kind = "error"; Reason = $_.Exception.Message }
312:     }
313:     if (-not $root) {
314:         return @{ Kind = "error"; Reason = "the path has no root" }
315:     }
316:     if ($full.Length -le ([string]$root).TrimEnd("\").Length) {
317:         return @{ Kind = "root" }
318:     }
319:     return @{ Kind = "ok"; Path = ($full + ".source-manifest") }
320: }
321: 
322: function Write-SourceManifestSidecar($path, $manifestLines) {
323:     # ADVISORY ONLY. It pins nothing and gates nothing, so a failure to
324:     # write it is not a build failure - it costs a later refusal its
325:     # explanation and nothing else. That is also why it may be a file at
326:     # all: the header's rule about values passed as arguments rather than
327:     # re-read from a file governs values that PIN something, and this
328:     # value carries no authority.
329:     #
330:     # CREATE-NEW, never WriteAllLines. State the guarantee exactly: it
331:     # makes the FINAL PATH COMPONENT safe, so a file created between the
332:     # guards and this write is not overwritten and a link substituted at
333:     # that name is not written through. It does NOT defend a DIRECTORY
334:     # component - an ancestor replaced by a junction before this open
335:     # redirects creation into that junction's target, and no open flag
336:     # prevents that. The alias guards above are what cover ancestors, and
337:     # they run before any of this.
338:     try {
339:         $utf8 = New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false)
340:         $fs = [System.IO.File]::Open($path, 'CreateNew', 'Write', 'None')
341:         try {
342:             $sw = New-Object System.IO.StreamWriter($fs, $utf8)
343:             try {
344:                 foreach ($line in @($manifestLines)) {
345:                     $sw.WriteLine([string]$line)
346:                 }
347:             } finally { $sw.Dispose() }
348:         } finally { $fs.Dispose() }
349:         return $true
350:     } catch {
351:         return $false
352:     }
353: }
354: ```
355: 
356: - [ ] **Step 5a: Resolve and lexically guard the destination, WITHOUT touching it**
357: 
358: Insert immediately AFTER the override's pre-existence refusal, the block ending `"a stale override reads exactly like a fresh one"` at `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:970-973`, and BEFORE the `PATH BUDGET PRE-FLIGHT` comment. `$rr`, `$mp`, `$op` and `$cmp` are the normalized paths and the comparison mode the override guard above already set up.
359: 
360: NOTHING HERE MUTATES ANYTHING. The removal is Step 5c, after every check has passed:
361: 
362: ```powershell
363: # THE ADVISORY SOURCE MANIFEST'S DESTINATION, resolved and LEXICALLY
364: # guarded here, beside the override, for the same stated reason: a
365: # destination discovered after the build has copied, remediated and
366: # manifested is discovered too late, and -SkipProbe would bypass a check
367: # placed later. Its ALIAS guards are further down with the override's,
368: # and its removal is after both, because a build that is going to be
369: # refused must not have deleted anything first.
370: $smResult = Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath $MirrorPath
371: if ($smResult.Kind -eq "root") {
372:     Write-Output ("ERROR: the mirror path is a filesystem root" +
373:         " ($MirrorPath), which has no sibling, so no advisory source" +
374:         " manifest can be placed beside it")
375:     exit 2
376: }
377: if ($smResult.Kind -ne "ok") {
378:     Write-Output ("ERROR: the mirror path could not be resolved in order to" +
379:         " place an advisory source manifest beside it ($MirrorPath): " +
380:         $smResult.Reason)
381:     exit 2
382: }
383: $SourceManifestOut = $smResult.Path
384: $smp = $SourceManifestOut.Replace("\", "/").TrimEnd("/")
385: foreach ($protected in @($rr, $mp)) {
386:     if (($smp + "/").Equals($protected, $cmp) -or
387:         ($smp + "/").StartsWith($protected, $cmp) -or
388:         $protected.StartsWith($smp + "/", $cmp)) {
389:         Write-Output ("ERROR: the source manifest path overlaps a protected" +
390:             " tree ($SourceManifestOut)")
391:         exit 2
392:     }
393: }
394: if ($smp.Equals($op, $cmp)) {
395:     Write-Output ("ERROR: the source manifest path is the override path" +
396:         " ($SourceManifestOut) - the advisory write would replace the file" +
397:         " the probe verified and the wrapper hashes")
398:     exit 2
399: }
400: if ($SourceManifestOut.Length -ge 260) {
401:     Write-Output ("ERROR: path budget exceeded by the source manifest - " +
402:         "$SourceManifestOut is $($SourceManifestOut.Length) characters " +
403:         "and the limit is 260")
404:     exit 2
405: }
406: ```
407: 
408: The literal `260` is used here because `$PathBudget` is assigned below this point. If the implementer prefers the variable, MOVE the `$PathBudget = 260` assignment above this block rather than moving this block down: this guard must stay ahead of every mutation.

428: - [ ] **Step 5c: Refuse or remove a pre-existing sidecar, AFTER all validation**
429: 
430: Insert immediately AFTER the followed-target overlap loop from Step 5b, so every lexical and alias check has already passed:
431: 
432: ```powershell
433: # LAST, because a build that is going to be refused must not have deleted
434: # anything first. Read the ATTRIBUTES rather than calling Test-Path: a
435: # dangling reparse point is not reliably reported as existing, which the
436: # link walker above documents, and this is the one place where a wrong
437: # "it is not there" turns into a write.
438: $smAttr = $null
439: try {
440:     $smAttr = [System.IO.File]::GetAttributes($SourceManifestOut)
441: } catch [System.IO.FileNotFoundException] {
442:     $smAttr = $null
443: } catch [System.IO.DirectoryNotFoundException] {
444:     $smAttr = $null
445: } catch {
446:     Write-Output ("ERROR: the source manifest path could not be examined" +
447:         " ($SourceManifestOut): " + $_.Exception.Message)
448:     exit 2
449: }
450: if ($null -ne $smAttr) {
451:     if (([int]$smAttr -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::Directory) -ne 0) {
452:         Write-Output ("ERROR: $SourceManifestOut is a directory - this tool" +
453:             " replaces a file there and never removes a tree")
454:         exit 2
455:     }
456:     if (-not $Force) {
457:         Write-Output ("ERROR: $SourceManifestOut already exists - pass" +
458:             " -Force to replace it, the same rule the mirror path follows")
459:         exit 2
460:     }
461:     # REMOVING a link removes the link and never its target's bytes,
462:     # which is exactly why the removal is safe where a write through it
463:     # was not.
464:     Remove-Item -LiteralPath $SourceManifestOut -Force
465: }
466: ```

468: - [ ] **Step 5b2: Refuse an extra input that collides with the sidecar**
469: 
470: The `-ExtraInput` parser at `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:908-925` runs BEFORE `$smp` exists, and it stores paths already resolved by `Resolve-Path` into `$ExtraInputPaths`. So the check belongs here, after Step 5b, where both `$smp` and `Test-PathOrAncestorIsLink` are available and nothing has been removed yet. Insert it immediately after Step 5b's second loop:
471: 
472: ```powershell
473: # EXTRA INPUTS versus the sidecar. $ExtraInputPaths holds paths already
474: # resolved by the -ExtraInput parser above; that parser runs before $smp
475: # exists, which is why this check lives here rather than beside it.
476: foreach ($eiResolved in @($ExtraInputPaths)) {
477:     $eiNorm = ([string]$eiResolved).Replace("\", "/").TrimEnd("/")
478:     if ($eiNorm.Equals($smp, $cmp)) {
479:         Write-Output ("ERROR: -ExtraInput '" + $eiResolved + "' is the" +
480:             " source manifest path, which this build replaces - a declared" +
481:             " review input must not be a file this tool overwrites")
482:         exit 2
483:     }
484:     # SPELLING EQUALITY IS NOT IDENTITY, and the comparison above is only
485:     # spelling. With C:\alias a junction to C:\out, a mirror at
486:     # C:\out\mirror and an extra input at
487:     # C:\alias\mirror.source-manifest, BOTH output paths have ordinary
488:     # ancestors and pass their own alias checks, while the extra input
489:     # keeps the alias spelling and compares unequal - and -Force would
490:     # then remove the very file that extra input names, after which the
491:     # unchecked Copy-Item can leave it out of the mirror entirely. This
492:     # tool cannot establish physical identity here, so it REFUSES the
493:     # case it cannot decide rather than guessing.
494:     $eiHit = Test-PathOrAncestorIsLink $eiResolved
495:     if ($eiHit) {
496:         Write-Output ("ERROR: -ExtraInput '" + $eiResolved + "' is reached" +
497:             " through a directory link at " + $eiHit + ", so this tool" +
498:             " cannot tell whether it names the same file as the source" +
499:             " manifest it replaces; pass the path behind the link")
500:         exit 2
501:     }
502: }
503: ```

560: ### Task 2: The source-status refusal names what moved
561: 
562: **Files:**
563: - Modify: `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1` (three new functions beside the sidecar functions, and the refusal at `:845-851`)
564: - Test: `evals/multi-model-verify/test_review_mirror.py`
565: 
566: **Interfaces:**
567: - Consumes: `Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath($mirrorPath)` and `Get-StatusSha256`'s `Manifest` field from Task 1.
568: - Produces:
569:   - `Read-BoundedRecords($text, $maxRecords)` returns `@{ Records = <string[]>; Truncated = <bool> }`, reading with a `StringReader` so the cap applies during extraction rather than after it.
570:   - `Get-ManifestDrift($recordedLines, $liveLines)` returns `@{ Entered = <string[]>; Left = <string[]>; Changed = <string[]>; Malformed = <int> }`. The three lists are sorted by path. `Malformed` counts every record the parser rejected.
571:   - `Format-AdvisoryName($name)` returns a string bounded at 200 characters, with every character in the Unicode categories `Control`, `Format`, `LineSeparator`, `ParagraphSeparator` and `Surrogate` rendered as `\uNNNN`. Those five and no others: `SpaceSeparator` is NOT escaped, so U+00A0 passes through, and an earlier draft's "every separator character" claimed otherwise.
572:   - `Write-SourceDriftExplanation($mirrorPath, $liveManifest)` writes lines to the pipeline. It is called for its output; its return value is not read.
573: 
574: **What the previous draft claimed and did not deliver.** Cross-vendor review confirmed four gaps in the second draft, each an instance of the class this whole cycle exists to remove: the parser accepted `bad.txt not-a-hash` as a valid record while the plan said malformed records were counted; `Get-Item` measured the file and a separate `ReadAllBytes` read it, so the size limit bounded nothing; `Format-AdvisoryName` tested `[int]$ch -lt 32` and so passed C1 controls such as U+0085 and U+009B straight through, along with U+2028 and U+202E; and the sidecar path and exception text bypassed the formatter entirely.
575:

686:     assert proc.returncode == 1, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
687:     assert "source status" in proc.stdout.lower(), proc.stdout
688:

690: def test_a_record_whose_hash_field_is_not_a_hash_is_counted(tmp_path):
691:     """`bad.txt not-a-hash` used to parse as an ordinary record, so a
692:     truncated digest became a reported difference rather than an
693:     admission that the explanation is incomplete."""
694:     repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
695:     mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"

699:                        + "empty.txt \n" + "no-space-here\n")

708:     """The split artifact is not a record. Counting it would put a

766: - [ ] **Step 3: Add the diff and rendering functions**
767: 
768: Insert into `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1` immediately after `Write-SourceManifestSidecar`:
769: 
770: ```powershell
771: function Get-ManifestDrift($recordedLines, $liveLines) {
772:     # Both sides are "<relpath> <sha256hex>" lines, so the LAST space is
773:     # the separator: a pathname may hold spaces and a hex digest may not.
774:     #
775:     # ORDINAL, CASE-SENSITIVE keys. A PowerShell hashtable compares keys
776:     # case-INsensitively, so `File.txt` and `file.txt` collapse into one
777:     # entry and a case-only rename reports no difference at all - while
778:     # the digest, built from the raw strings, changes. The explanation
779:     # must partition the same strings the digest does.
780:     #
781:     # THE GRAMMAR IS CHECKED, not assumed. A record whose hash field is
782:     # not 64 lowercase hex characters is malformed, and so is a duplicate
783:     # key, an over-long line, and a line with no separator. Every one is
784:     # COUNTED, never silently dropped: a dropped record is a difference
785:     # this explanation would then fail to mention, which is the shape of
786:     # defect this whole change exists to remove. An EMPTY line is the
787:     # split artifact of a trailing newline and is not a record at all, so
788:     # it is skipped without counting.
789:     $ord = [System.StringComparer]::Ordinal
790:     $recorded = New-Object "System.Collections.Generic.Dictionary[string,string]" $ord
791:     $live = New-Object "System.Collections.Generic.Dictionary[string,string]" $ord
792:     $malformed = 0
793:     $hexRx = '^[0-9a-f]{64}$'
794:     foreach ($side in @(@($recordedLines, $recorded), @($liveLines, $live))) {
795:         foreach ($line in @($side[0])) {
796:             $s = [string]$line
797:             # AN EMPTY LINE IS A MALFORMED RECORD, with no special case
798:             # for a trailing one: the caller reads records with a
799:             # StringReader, which returns nothing at all for a file
800:             # ending in a newline, so every empty string that reaches
801:             # here is a real empty record. The draft that skipped every
802:             # empty string silently dropped leading and interior ones.
803:             #
804:             # THERE IS NO RECORD CAP HERE. Capping mid-loop turned
805:             # resource exhaustion into a grammar diagnosis: at record
806:             # 200,001 the draft incremented Malformed once and abandoned
807:             # the rest, so 200,003 malformed records reported 200,001.
808:             # The cap belongs to the reader, which reports it as its own
809:             # state.
810:             if ($s.Length -eq 0) { $malformed++; continue }
811:             if ($s.Length -gt 4096) { $malformed++; continue }
812:             $cut = $s.LastIndexOf(" ")
813:             if ($cut -lt 1) { $malformed++; continue }
814:             $hex = $s.Substring($cut + 1)
815:             if ($hex -cnotmatch $hexRx) { $malformed++; continue }
816:             $key = $s.Substring(0, $cut)
817:             if ($side[1].ContainsKey($key)) { $malformed++; continue }
818:             $side[1][$key] = $hex
819:         }
820:     }
821:     $entered = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
822:     $left = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
823:     $changed = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
824:     foreach ($p in @($live.Keys)) {
825:         if (-not $recorded.ContainsKey($p)) {
826:             [void]$entered.Add($p)
827:         } elseif ($recorded[$p] -ne $live[$p]) {
828:             [void]$changed.Add($p)
829:         }
830:     }
831:     foreach ($p in @($recorded.Keys)) {
832:         if (-not $live.ContainsKey($p)) { [void]$left.Add($p) }
833:     }
834:     return @{ Entered   = @($entered | Sort-Object)
835:               Left      = @($left    | Sort-Object)
836:               Changed   = @($changed | Sort-Object)
837:               Malformed = $malformed }
838: }
839: 
840: function Read-BoundedRecords($text, $maxRecords) {
841:     # Returns @{ Records = <string[]>; Truncated = <bool> }.
842:     #
843:     # A StringReader, never `-split`. The split materializes every line of
844:     # a file whose size is someone else's choice BEFORE any cap can apply,
845:     # so a cap placed after it bounds nothing that matters. Reading line
846:     # by line stops AT the cap, having built only that many strings.
847:     #
848:     # It also removes the trailing-newline special case rather than
849:     # handling it: ReadLine returns $null at end of input, so a file
850:     # ending in a newline yields no final empty string. Every empty line
851:     # this returns is therefore a real empty record, which is what lets
852:     # the parser count one as malformed instead of guessing which empties
853:     # were artifacts.
854:     #
855:     # Truncation is its OWN state. It is not a malformed record and must
856:     # never be reported as one: the remainder was not examined, which is
857:     # a different fact about a different set of records.
858:     $out = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
859:     $truncated = $false
860:     $reader = New-Object System.IO.StringReader($text)
861:     try {
862:         while ($true) {
863:             $line = $reader.ReadLine()
864:             if ($null -eq $line) { break }
865:             if ($out.Count -ge $maxRecords) { $truncated = $true; break }
866:             [void]$out.Add($line)
867:         }
868:     } finally { $reader.Dispose() }
869:     return @{ Records = @($out); Truncated = $truncated }
870: }
871: 
872: function Format-AdvisoryName($name) {
873:     # EVERY untrusted string printed by the explanation goes through
874:     # here, pathnames and exception text alike. The advisory manifest is
875:     # MUTABLE, so nothing in it passed Test-SupportedPathname.
876:     #
877:     # The test is the UNICODE CATEGORY, never a numeric range. Measured
878:     # 2026-09-05: `[int]$ch -lt 32` passes the C1 controls U+0085 and
879:     # U+009B, which are terminal escape introducers, and it passes
880:     # U+2028 and the bidirectional override U+202E, which manipulate how
881:     # the rest of the line displays. Control, Format, LineSeparator and
882:     # ParagraphSeparator cover all of them by name.
883:     $s = [string]$name
884:     if ($s.Length -gt 200) { $s = $s.Substring(0, 200) + "[truncated]" }
885:     $sb = New-Object System.Text.StringBuilder
886:     foreach ($ch in $s.ToCharArray()) {
887:         $cat = [System.Globalization.CharUnicodeInfo]::GetUnicodeCategory($ch)
888:         if (($cat -eq [System.Globalization.UnicodeCategory]::Control) -or
889:             ($cat -eq [System.Globalization.UnicodeCategory]::Format) -or
890:             ($cat -eq [System.Globalization.UnicodeCategory]::LineSeparator) -or
891:             ($cat -eq [System.Globalization.UnicodeCategory]::ParagraphSeparator) -or
892:             ($cat -eq [System.Globalization.UnicodeCategory]::Surrogate)) {
893:             [void]$sb.Append("\u" + ([int]$ch).ToString("x4"))
894:         } else {
895:             [void]$sb.Append($ch)
896:         }
897:     }
898:     return $sb.ToString()
899: }
900: ```
901: 
902: **Why `Entered` and `Left` rather than `Appeared` and `Vanished`.** Manifest membership is not file existence. `Get-ManifestSubject` omits a deletion-only entry because it has no bytes (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:460-463`), and a clean tracked file that becomes dirty ENTERS the manifest without being created. Both files can exist the whole time. The labels name what the comparison actually measures.

909: function Write-SourceDriftExplanation($mirrorPath, $liveManifest) {
910:     # Runs ONLY after the source-status refusal below has been printed.
911:     #
912:     # THE WHOLE BODY IS WRAPPED. What protects the verdict is not any
913:     # property of this function - Write-Output emits into the pipeline
914:     # like any other command, and a caller COULD capture it - but the
915:     # fact that its one caller ignores the output and reaches `exit 1`

924:         }

The region is about 301. Putting it in `SKILL.md` breaches the ceiling by an order of magnitude more than the space available, and a prior cycle already stashed a Task 3 at 6609 tokens for exactly this reason.

1067: - [ ] **Step 1: Write the region into the reference**
1068: 
1069: Append this block to `skills/multi-model-verify/references/preflight-mirror.md`, at column zero, under a new `## The quiet period` heading placed before Task 4's `## Timing` section:
1070: 
1071: ```markdown
1072: <!-- contract:start id=mirror-quiet-period -->
1073: NOTHING MAY WRITE INSIDE THE REVIEWED REPOSITORY from the moment the
1074: mirror is built until the wrapper exits. The identity digest covers the
1075: fields of `git status --porcelain --ignored` PLUS the content of the
1076: paths that listing names, ignored ones included, with a directory
1077: expanded to its files and a deletion-only entry contributing no bytes.
1078: So a test-cache write, a plan-ledger append, a drift report or one new
1079: untracked file is enough. The same recorded digest is compared against
1080: the live source three times: at preparation, before the client runs,
1081: and after it finishes. The last of those spans the whole round, so this
1082: is a quiet period and not an ordering rule. Only that last one costs a
1083: reviewer round; the two before it refuse before the client is invoked
1084: and spend no quota. State the limits with the rule: the comparison
1085: samples endpoints, so a change made and reverted inside the round is
1086: not detected, and a tracked file git reports CLEAN is covered by
1087: neither fingerprint. Queue every edit until the wrapper exits, however
1088: small and however unrelated it looks.
1089: <!-- contract:end -->
1090: ```

1092: - [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing pin**
1093: 
1094: Add to `evals/multi-model-verify/test_multi_model_verify.py`, in `TestSkillStructure` directly after `test_client_context_probe_failure_rule_is_pinned`:
1095: 
1096: ```python
1097:     def test_mirror_quiet_period_is_pinned(self):

1213: Append to `skills/multi-model-verify/references/preflight-mirror.md`, as a new final section:
1214: 
1215: ```markdown
1216: ## Timing
1217: 
1218: BUILD THE MIRROR LAST. Every act that writes inside the reviewed
1219: repository finishes first: the gates, the plan ledger, the scratch notes,
1220: the formatter. From the build until the round's wrapper exits, the
1221: repository is quiet, and SKILL.md's `mirror-quiet-period` region states
1222: why. The identity digest covers the content of ignored paths, so a
1223: pytest cache directory or a ledger append is enough to refuse the
1224: dispatch.
1225: 
1226: A build that has gone stale is not repaired and cannot be re-blessed:
1227: there is deliberately no re-mint or reseal mode. READ THE EXPLANATION
1228: FIRST, then build again. Rebuilding replaces the evidence of what
1229: changed, so a rebuild before reading turns a diagnosable refusal into an
1230: unexplained one. The rebuild itself is cheap next to a spent round,
1231: measured at about 92 seconds on a repo carrying a linked reference
1232: checkout.
1233: 
1234: When a refusal names `the source status changed since construction`, the
1235: lines beneath it name the paths whose content changed and the paths that
1236: entered or left manifest coverage, read from the `source_manifest` file
1237: the record block points at. That explanation is advisory: it can be
1238: missing, incomplete or wrong, and the refusal stands either way.
1239: Manifest coverage is not file existence, so a path listed as leaving
1240: coverage has not necessarily been deleted.
1241: 
1242: The two refusals raised by the round wrapper itself print no explanation
1243: to the console. The wrapper redirects both identity checks into
1244: `mirror.verify` inside its dispatch directory and then throws a short
1245: message, so that file is where the detail is.
1246: ```

1257: 
1258: ```markdown
1259: ## 94. The identity digest covers working state that moves on its own
1260: Status: OPEN
1261: Cost: every round is exposed for its whole duration to a write into an ignored working directory it does not control, and a write that persists past the post-client check costs one round of quota
1262: Pairs: 76, 91

1264: 
1265: **Filed 2026-09-05 from a report by a session running the plugin against
1266: another repository**, which hit repeated
1267: `BLOCKED: the source status changed since construction` refusals and
1268: could not tell why. Design:
1269: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window-design.md`.
1270: 
1271: `Get-StatusSha256` fingerprints that status listing's own fields plus
1272: the content of the paths it names, with `Get-ContentManifest` expanding
1273: a directory subject recursively, so one `!! .claude/` entry pulls in
1274: every file beneath it. A deletion-only entry has no bytes and
1275: contributes none. The content half is deliberate and
1276: load-bearing: editing an already-ignored file leaves the status listing
1277: byte-identical, so a list-only digest would pass through exactly the
1278: tampering the check exists to catch.
1279: 
1280: The exposure is the WHOLE ROUND, not the preparation gap. The recorded
1281: digest is compared three times, and the third comparison runs after the
1282: client finishes. In a repository whose ignored directories move on their
1283: own, no ordering discipline closes that.
1284: 
1285: **What this cycle did and did not do.** The refusal now names the paths
1286: that moved, the skill states the quiet period, and the reference orders
1287: the build last. None of that narrows the digest, so the underlying
1288: exposure is unchanged and this item stays OPEN.
1289: 
1290: **A candidate fix, rejected for now.** A declared allowlist of volatile
1291: ignored paths held outside the content digest. Rejected because the
1292: directories that move on their own, `.claude/` and `.codex/`, are
1293: instruction surfaces, and excluding an instruction surface from the
1294: tamper net defeats the check. Closing this item means a design that
1295: narrows the digest without opening that hole, argued in the
1296: `mirror-identity-gate` contract region and debated on its own.
1297: ```

1301: The plan debate's reviewer swept the class it was asked to sweep and found defects that PREDATE this range. The debate protocol's scope rule says to RECORD anything not of the same class on the same verification surface, so they are filed rather than fixed. Append after item 94:
1302: 
1303: ```markdown
1304: ## 95. Three stated properties of the mirror tools that the code does not hold
1305: Status: OPEN
1306: Cost: each one is a promise a reader relies on, and one of them can leave an extra input missing from a mirror the digest then certifies
1307: Pairs: 94
1308: Verified: <run the digest command in Step 6 and paste the value here>
1309: 
1310: **Filed 2026-09-05 from the plan debate for item 94's cycle**, whose
1311: reviewer was asked to sweep for stated properties the code does not hold.
1312: Record:
1313: `docs/superpowers/plans/rounds/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window/`.
1314: All three were confirmed against the code by the session before filing.
1315: 
1316: 1. **The header's absolute promise.** `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:19`
1317:    says the script never writes to the real tree. The directory-link
1318:    guard at `:1101-1112` says git's optional index refresh writes the
1319:    repository's own `.git/index` during every status capture, and refuses
1320:    a linked `.git` for exactly that reason. Qualify the promise or
1321:    suppress the write.
1322: 
1323: 2. **`-ExtraInput` can fail silently.** `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1554`
1324:    copies each extra input with `Copy-Item -Force`, with no success check
1325:    and no `-ErrorAction Stop`, and the script never sets
1326:    `$ErrorActionPreference` (its own comment at `:519` says so). A
1327:    non-terminating copy failure leaves the input absent, and the manifest
1328:    cannot discover a file that never entered status. The tool then
1329:    certifies a mirror missing a declared review input.
1330: 
1331: 3. **The verify's same-directory refusal checks spelling.** The
1332:    comparison at `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:734-745` normalizes two
1333:    provider-resolved strings and compares them, with none of the
1334:    reparse-point resolution the build performs. A junction whose
1335:    spelling differs can reach the same directory, so the refusal is
1336:    narrower than the comment claims.
1337: 
1338: 4. **Construction claims an endpoint comparison cannot support.** The
1339:    comment at `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1308` says the retained value
1340:    distinguishes a source that moved and moved back. Two equal endpoint
1341:    measurements cannot establish that. The identity contract already
1342:    admits that intermediate bytes can reach the copy despite matching
1343:    endpoints; this comment has not been aligned with it.
1344: 
1345: 5. **A wrapper verification failure cannot report the classification it
1346:    promises.** Both identity checks in the generated wrapper `throw`
1347:    (`tools/dispatch-round.ps1:287` and `:311`), which exits before
1348:    `-Classify` runs, so the documented exit map does not describe that
1349:    path.
1350: 
1351: **Corroborated by the plan debate's reviewer with citations, NOT
1352: independently re-checked by the session.** Confirm each before acting on
1353: it: the generated wrapper is written as ASCII so a non-ASCII path
1354: component is lost (`tools/dispatch-round.ps1:570`); the printed command
1355: places paths in expandable double-quoted strings without escaping `$`
1356: (`:591`); `classifying:<nonce>` redemption is a read-then-write with no
1357: exclusive reservation (`:663`); a receipt write that fails after creation
1358: leaves a partial receipt against a documented "no receipt" (`:572`); and
1359: an oversized integer in `exit` passes the regex and throws during
1360: conversion instead of following the classification map (`:794`).
1361: 
1362: **One earlier report is NOT filed here, deliberately.** The voided round
1363: raised `Test-SupportedPathname` admitting U+FEFF as a defect. It is not
1364: one on its own: it mattered only because the first draft's advisory
1365: reader detected and consumed a byte-order mark, and Task 2 replaces that
1366: reader with explicit byte decoding. Admitting U+FEFF in a pathname is
1367: correct behaviour.
1368: ```

1443: 
1444: ## Self-review
1445: 
1446: **Spec coverage.** D1 (no gate change) is carried by the Global Constraints and by Task 2's two advisory-direction tests. D2 and D2b (the advisory explanation and its bounds) are Tasks 1 and 2. D2a (the destination guards, in both the lexical and the alias block, validating before removing) is Task 1 Steps 5a, 5b and 5c. D3 (quiet period in the skill) is Task 3. D4 (build ordered last) is Task 4. Every success criterion in the spec has a named test in Task 1 Step 1 or Task 2 Step 1, except the six-tier and two-host criteria, which are Task 5.
1447: 
1448: **Placeholders.** Two values are deliberately left to be computed rather than guessed: the `Verified:` digests for items 94 and 95, in Task 4 Step 6, because each is a hash of its own item's final text and cannot exist before that text does. The command that produces them is given.
1449: 
1450: **Type consistency.** `Get-StatusSha256` gains `Manifest` in Task 1 and Task 2 reads `$liveStatus.Manifest`. `Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath` is defined in Task 1 and called by Task 1's guard block and by Task 2's `Write-SourceDriftExplanation`; it returns `$null` for a root and both callers handle that. `Get-ManifestDrift` returns `Entered`, `Left`, `Changed` and `Malformed`, and `Write-SourceDriftExplanation` reads exactly those four. `Format-AdvisoryName` is defined in Task 2 Step 3 and called in Step 4 for names, for the sidecar path and for exception text. The sidecar path `"<full mirror path>.source-manifest"` is derived in Task 1 Step 4 and asserted as `tmp_path / "mirror.source-manifest"` in both tasks' tests.
1451: 
1452: ## After the plan
1453: 
1454: This plan does NOT bump `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`. Per `CLAUDE.md`, the bump comes after the diff debate, because the debate is what moves the tree last. The sequence after Task 5 is: whole-branch review, mode-diff debate, version bump, merge, marketplace refresh, plugin update, and an install verified by content rather than by the cache directory's name.

 succeeded in 325ms:
1: """Review mirror construction (0.17.0, backlog item 4).
2: 
3: The mirror is a FILE COPY preserving .git, never a clone: the review inputs
4: are routinely gitignored, and a clone carries tracked files only. Probed
5: 2026-07-26 in KitnEssentials, where a cloned workspace dropped the frozen
6: plan, the spec and the reference source while every route and containment
7: check stayed green.
8: """
9: import hashlib
10: import json
11: import os
12: import re
13: import shutil
14: import stat
15: import subprocess
16: import tempfile
17: from pathlib import Path
18: 
19: import pytest
20: 
21: REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
22: MIRROR = REPO_ROOT / "tools" / "new-review-mirror.ps1"
23: FIXTURES = Path(__file__).parent / "fixtures" / "codex-prompt-input"
24: STUB = Path(__file__).parent / "fixtures" / "stub-codex" / "stub-codex.ps1"
25: 
26: 
27: # WINDOWS ONLY, for the same reason as the probe's suite: the mirror runs
28: # the probe, which resolves the reviewer's global instruction file under
29: # `$env:USERPROFILE`, and the lane lock lives under `$env:LOCALAPPDATA`.
30: # Neither exists on Linux, so the run refuses to report a measurement it
31: # could not make - the script working, not failing.
32: #
33: # Guarding only on a PowerShell host being present was not enough: the CI
34: # runner ships `pwsh`, so these cases ran on Linux and failed there for a
35: # second, different reason. See the probe suite's header for the full
36: # sequence.
37: #
38: # COVERAGE, recorded rather than assumed: these cases are Windows-only, so
39: # the ubuntu job skips them. The `powershell-hosts` job on windows-latest
40: # runs this module under BOTH powershell.exe and pwsh.exe. If this module
41: # ever leaves both Windows pytest steps, that claim is false and
42: # test_backup_lane.py asserts it back.
43: POWERSHELL = (os.environ.get("PARALLAX_PS_HOST")
44:               or shutil.which("powershell") or shutil.which("pwsh"))
45: 
46: pytestmark = pytest.mark.skipif(
47:     os.name != "nt" or POWERSHELL is None,
48:     reason="the review mirror is a Windows tool: it needs a PowerShell "
49:            "host and the Windows profile variables it measures")
50: 
51: 
52: def ps_host():
53:     return POWERSHELL
54: 
55: 
56: def git(repo, *args):
57:     return subprocess.run(["git", "-C", str(repo), *args],
58:                           capture_output=True, text=True, check=True).stdout
59: 
60: 
61: def commit(repo, message):
62:     git(repo, "-c", "user.email=t@t", "-c", "user.name=t",
63:         "commit", "-q", "-m", message)
64: 
65: 
66: def make_repo(tmp_path):
67:     """A scratch repo with one tracked file, one ignored file, and one
68:     untracked file, so the baseline has something to carry."""
69:     repo = tmp_path / "src"
70:     repo.mkdir()
71:     git(tmp_path, "init", "-q", str(repo))
72:     (repo / "kept.txt").write_text("tracked\n")
73:     (repo / ".gitignore").write_text("ignored/\n")
74:     (repo / "ignored").mkdir()
75:     (repo / "ignored" / "secret.txt").write_text("gitignored input\n")
76:     (repo / "untracked.txt").write_text("untracked\n")
77:     git(repo, "add", "kept.txt", ".gitignore")
78:     commit(repo, "base")
79:     return repo
80: 
81: 
82: def make_clean_repo(tmp_path):
83:     """A repo with NOTHING for the baseline to carry: no back-channels, no
84:     untracked files, no ignored files. The ordinary case, and the one an
85:     empty-array return would have misread as an enumeration failure."""
86:     repo = tmp_path / "clean"
87:     repo.mkdir()
88:     git(tmp_path, "init", "-q", str(repo))
89:     (repo / "only.txt").write_text("tracked\n")
90:     git(repo, "add", "only.txt")
91:     commit(repo, "base")
92:     return repo
93: 
94: 
95: def run_mirror(repo, mirror, *extra, env=None):
96:     """`env` carries the build's three TEST SEAMS.
97: 
98:     They are environment variables, not parameters, for the reason the
99:     lane-home builder states about its own two: a parameter is public
100:     surface on a shipped tool. No shipped caller sets either.
101: 
102:     Each is a BOOLEAN that ORs one extra block condition into a
103:     comparison. It cannot supply a value, so it cannot suppress a real
104:     measurement, and setting it can only ever ADD a reason to fail.
105:     That is what makes the one-way claim structural rather than a
106:     property of the value someone happens to pass.
107: 
108:     Two earlier shapes failed that bar and the whole-branch review
109:     caught both. The first copied from a different tree and committed
110:     into the real source repo. The second INJECTED a value, so a parent
111:     that set the variable to the repo's own current HEAD - trivially
112:     readable in advance - suppressed the genuine post-copy measurement
113:     and turned a build that should have failed into one that passed.
114:     """
115:     full = None
116:     if env:
117:         full = dict(os.environ)
118:         full.update(env)
119:     return subprocess.run(
120:         [ps_host(), "-NoProfile", "-NonInteractive", "-File", str(MIRROR),
121:          "-RepoRoot", str(repo), "-MirrorPath", str(mirror),
122:          "-SkipProbe", *extra],
123:         capture_output=True, text=True, env=full)
124: 
125: 
126: SKIP_BLOCK = "BLOCKED: no client measurement was made (-SkipProbe)"
127: 
128: 
129: def assert_built(proc):
130:     """-SkipProbe builds the mirror but VERIFIES nothing, so it exits 1.
131: 
132:     The exit code reports dispatch-readiness, not construction: a mirror
133:     with no client measurement is not cleared for dispatch, and must not
134:     share an exit code with one that is. The mode-diff review of
135:     2026-07-28 found the old exit 0 there.
136: 
137:     Asserting the SPECIFIC skip line is what keeps these tests able to
138:     catch a real construction failure, which also exits 1 but with a
139:     different reason.
140:     """
141:     assert proc.returncode == 1, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
142:     assert SKIP_BLOCK in proc.stdout, proc.stdout + proc.stderr
143: 
144: 
145: def make_junction(link, target):
146:     """A junction via mklink /J, or skip. Junctions need no privilege;
147:     symbolic links do, and this session cannot make one."""
148:     rc = subprocess.run(["cmd", "/c", "mklink", "/J", str(link), str(target)],
149:                         capture_output=True, text=True)
150:     if rc.returncode != 0:
151:         pytest.skip("junction creation unavailable: " + rc.stderr)
152: 
153: 
154: def unlink_junction(link):
155:     """rmdir removes the junction and never its target."""
156:     subprocess.run(["cmd", "/c", "rmdir", str(link)],
157:                    capture_output=True, text=True, check=True)
158: 
159: 
160: def is_reparse_point(path):
161:     """Python's is_symlink() is False for a junction, so read the
162:     attribute bit the filesystem sets for both kinds of link."""
163:     return bool(os.lstat(str(path)).st_file_attributes
164:                 & stat.FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT)
165: 
166: 
167: def read_block(stdout, label):
168:     """Lines of one labelled block from the record output."""
169:     lines = stdout.splitlines()
170:     start = next(i for i, line in enumerate(lines) if line.startswith(label))
171:     out = []
172:     for line in lines[start + 1:]:
173:         if line and not line.startswith("  "):
174:             break
175:         if line.strip():
176:             out.append(line.strip())
177:     return out
178: 
179: 
180: def test_the_mirror_carries_gitignored_files(tmp_path):
181:     repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
182:     mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
183:     proc = run_mirror(repo, mirror)
184:     assert_built(proc)
185:     assert (mirror / "ignored" / "secret.txt").exists(), (
186:         "a clone would have dropped this; the mirror must not"
187:     )
188:     assert (mirror / ".git").exists()
189: 
190: 
191: def test_a_tracked_agents_md_is_deleted_and_committed(tmp_path):
192:     repo = make_repo(tmp_path)
193:     (repo / "AGENTS.md").write_text("# planted\n")
194:     git(repo, "add", "AGENTS.md")
195:     commit(repo, "plant")
196:     before = git(repo, "rev-parse", "HEAD").strip()
197:     mirror = tmp_path / "mirror"
198:     proc = run_mirror(repo, mirror)
199:     assert_built(proc)
200:     assert not (mirror / "AGENTS.md").exists()
201:     assert (repo / "AGENTS.md").exists(), "the real tree is never touched"
202:     after = git(mirror, "rev-parse", "HEAD").strip()
203:     assert after != before, (
204:         "a tracked deletion left uncommitted is a tracked modification in"
205:         " the baseline, which bars mode diff and breaks"
206:         " HEAD-identifies-content"
207:     )
208:     assert "AGENTS.md" not in git(mirror, "status", "--porcelain")

1317:    says the script never writes to the real tree. The directory-link
1318:    guard at `:1101-1112` says git's optional index refresh writes the
1319:    repository's own `.git/index` during every status capture, and refuses
1320:    a linked `.git` for exactly that reason. Qualify the promise or
1321:    suppress the write.
1322: 
1323: 2. **`-ExtraInput` can fail silently.** `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1554`
1324:    copies each extra input with `Copy-Item -Force`, with no success check
1325:    and no `-ErrorAction Stop`, and the script never sets
1326:    `$ErrorActionPreference` (its own comment at `:519` says so). A
1327:    non-terminating copy failure leaves the input absent, and the manifest
1328:    cannot discover a file that never entered status. The tool then
1329:    certifies a mirror missing a declared review input.
1330: 
1331: 3. **The verify's same-directory refusal checks spelling.** The
1332:    comparison at `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:734-745` normalizes two
1333:    provider-resolved strings and compares them, with none of the
1334:    reparse-point resolution the build performs. A junction whose
1335:    spelling differs can reach the same directory, so the refusal is
1336:    narrower than the comment claims.
1337: 
1338: 4. **Construction claims an endpoint comparison cannot support.** The
1339:    comment at `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1308` says the retained value
1340:    distinguishes a source that moved and moved back. Two equal endpoint
1341:    measurements cannot establish that. The identity contract already
1342:    admits that intermediate bytes can reach the copy despite matching
1343:    endpoints; this comment has not been aligned with it.
1344:

1346:    promises.** Both identity checks in the generated wrapper `throw`
1347:    (`tools/dispatch-round.ps1:287` and `:311`), which exits before
1348:    `-Classify` runs, so the documented exit map does not describe that
1349:    path.
1350:

1352: independently re-checked by the session.** Confirm each before acting on
1353: it: the generated wrapper is written as ASCII so a non-ASCII path
1354: component is lost (`tools/dispatch-round.ps1:570`); the printed command
1355: places paths in expandable double-quoted strings without escaping `$`
1356: (`:591`); `classifying:<nonce>` redemption is a read-then-write with no
1357: exclusive reservation (`:663`); a receipt write that fails after creation
1358: leaves a partial receipt against a documented "no receipt" (`:572`); and
1359: an oversized integer in `exit` passes the regex and throws during
1360: conversion instead of following the classification map (`:794`).

1444: ## Self-review
1445: 
1446: **Spec coverage.** D1 (no gate change) is carried by the Global Constraints and by Task 2's two advisory-direction tests. D2 and D2b (the advisory explanation and its bounds) are Tasks 1 and 2. D2a (the destination guards, in both the lexical and the alias block, validating before removing) is Task 1 Steps 5a, 5b and 5c. D3 (quiet period in the skill) is Task 3. D4 (build ordered last) is Task 4. Every success criterion in the spec has a named test in Task 1 Step 1 or Task 2 Step 1, except the six-tier and two-host criteria, which are Task 5.
1447: 
1448: **Placeholders.** Two values are deliberately left to be computed rather than guessed: the `Verified:` digests for items 94 and 95, in Task 4 Step 6, because each is a hash of its own item's final text and cannot exist before that text does. The command that produces them is given.
1449: 
1450: **Type consistency.** `Get-StatusSha256` gains `Manifest` in Task 1 and Task 2 reads `$liveStatus.Manifest`. `Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath` is defined in Task 1 and called by Task 1's guard block and by Task 2's `Write-SourceDriftExplanation`; it returns `$null` for a root and both callers handle that. `Get-ManifestDrift` returns `Entered`, `Left`, `Changed` and `Malformed`, and `Write-SourceDriftExplanation` reads exactly those four. `Format-AdvisoryName` is defined in Task 2 Step 3 and called in Step 4 for names, for the sidecar path and for exception text. The sidecar path `"<full mirror path>.source-manifest"` is derived in Task 1 Step 4 and asserted as `tmp_path / "mirror.source-manifest"` in both tasks' tests.

The sequence after Task 5 is: whole-branch review, mode-diff debate, version bump, merge, marketplace refresh, plugin update, and an install verified by content rather than by the cache directory's name.
