exec
"C:\\Program Files\\PowerShell\\7\\pwsh.exe" -Command '$p='"'docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window-design.md'; "'$a=Get-Content -LiteralPath $p; for($i=0;$i -lt $a.Count;$i++){ '"'{0}: {1}' -f ("'$i+1),$a[$i] }' in C:\Temp\pxr3
 succeeded in 172ms:
1: # Design: the review mirror's identity window
2: 
3: **Filed 2026-09-05.** Reported from a session running the plugin against
4: KitnEssentials, which hit repeated `BLOCKED: the source status changed
5: since construction` refusals and could not tell why.
6: 
7: ## The measured facts
8: 
9: 1. `Get-StatusSha256` (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:662`) fingerprints a
10:    repo as the fields of `git status --porcelain --ignored -uall -z`
11:    PLUS the content manifest of the paths that listing names. Not every
12:    path: `Get-ManifestSubject` omits a deletion-only entry, which has no
13:    bytes to hash.
14:    `Get-ContentManifest` (`:576`) expands a directory subject
15:    recursively, so a single `!! .claude/` status entry pulls the bytes of
16:    every file beneath it into the digest.
17: 2. The content half is deliberate and the function says so: editing an
18:    already-ignored file leaves the status listing byte-identical, so a
19:    list-only digest would pass through exactly the tampering the check
20:    exists to catch.
21: 3. The build-time `source_status_sha256` is re-checked at THREE points
22:    against the same recorded value:
23:    - `-Prepare` step 1a, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:457`
24:    - the wrapper, before the client runs, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:275`
25:    - the wrapper, after the client finishes,
26:      `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:310`, `throw 'the mirror changed while the
27:      round ran'`
28: 4. Therefore the exposed window is **mirror build to wrapper exit**, not
29:    build to prepare. It spans the whole round.
30: 5. The refusal names no path. `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:846` prints the
31:    class of change and stops.
32: 6. Nothing in `skills/` states the constraint. This repo's `CLAUDE.md`
33:    carries a quiet-period rule covering `-Prepare` to wrapper exit only,
34:    and says in its own text that no prose rule in `skills/` carries it.
35:    The plugin therefore does not take the rule to the repos it is
36:    installed into.
37: 
38: ## What the reporting session proposed, and why it is not the fix
39: 
40: The report proposed fusing the mirror build and `-Prepare` into one
41: command. That fusion is CONSTRUCTIBLE: one script could build, read the
42: probe's reported override hash, generate the wrapper body from it, and
43: then prepare. An earlier draft of this document called it impossible,
44: which was wrong, and cross-vendor review of that draft said so.
45: 
46: It is refused on a different ground. It closes the first of three windows
47: and leaves the largest one open. A round that takes ten minutes still
48: voids if an ignored file moves while the reviewer reads. Buying the small
49: window at the cost of a more complex build, while the round-length window
50: stays exactly as it was, is not the trade this defect calls for.
51: 
52: The ordering the fusion would have to respect is real and worth writing
53: down, because it constrains any future attempt: the build runs the client
54: context probe, the probe writes the verified override file, and the
55: wrapper body embeds that file's SHA256
56: (`skills/multi-model-verify/SKILL.md`, the `verified-override-dispatch`
57: region). So the build must complete before the wrapper body can exist,
58: and the body must exist before `-Prepare` can install it.
59: 
60: The real constraint is a QUIET PERIOD over the reviewed repository, from
61: the build until the wrapper exits.
62: 
63: ## Decisions
64: 
65: **D1. The gate does not change.** No path is removed from the digest, no
66: carve-out is added, and no re-mint or reseal mode is introduced. A
67: volatile-path allowlist was considered and rejected for this cycle: the
68: directories that move on their own (`.claude/`, `.codex/`) are instruction
69: surfaces, and excluding an instruction surface from the tamper net defeats
70: the check. It is recorded as an open backlog item, not built.
71: 
72: **D2. The refusal explains itself, advisorily.** The build writes the
73: SOURCE side's content manifest to a SIBLING of the mirror directory,
74: `<MirrorPath>.source-manifest`. On the source-status refusal path only,
75: `-VerifyIdentity` reads that file and names the paths whose content
76: changed and the paths that entered or left manifest coverage.
77: 
78: The advisory file is safe by construction and must stay that way:
79: 
80: - It is read ONLY after the comparison has already decided to block, so
81:   it can never turn a refusal into a pass. A missing, unreadable,
82:   truncated, or deliberately corrupted file changes the printed
83:   explanation and never the exit code.
84: - It is a sibling, not a child, of the mirror and lives outside the
85:   repository, so it enters neither `source_status_sha256` nor
86:   `mirror_state_sha256`.
87: - It pins nothing, which is why it may be a file at all. The header's
88:   rule that identity values are passed as arguments rather than re-read
89:   from a file the build wrote applies to values that PIN something; this
90:   one carries no authority.
91: 
92: **D2a. Its DESTINATION joins BOTH of the guard blocks `-OverrideOut`
93: already sits in, and nothing is removed until they all pass.** Cross-vendor review of the first draft found that writing to a
94: derived path with no guard is itself a write into an unknown file: a hard
95: link or symbolic link at that path would carry the write through to its
96: target, and a target inside the repository would be corrupted by the very
97: function that exists to explain a corrupted repository. The same review
98: found that an explicitly supplied `-OverrideOut <MirrorPath>.source-
99: manifest` passes today's checks and would then be overwritten, breaking
100: the wrapper's override hash.
101: 
102: The default override path is already a sibling of exactly this shape,
103: `<MirrorPath>.skills-override.txt`. Its protection sits in TWO places,
104: and round 1 of this plan's debate found that a draft claiming to copy
105: "the override's whole guard set" had copied only the first:
106: 
107: - the LEXICAL block (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:951-1008`): overlap
108:   with the repository and the mirror, pre-existence, path budget;
109: - the ALIAS block (`:1258-1290`): walk the path's ancestors for a
110:   reparse point, and check it against every followed link target.
111: 
112: The sidecar joins both, plus an overlap check against the override path
113: and against every declared `-ExtraInput`.
114: 
115: **The extra-input check refuses what it cannot decide.** Spelling
116: equality is not filesystem identity. With `C:\alias` a junction to
117: `C:\out`, a mirror at `C:\out\mirror` and an extra input at
118: `C:\alias\mirror.source-manifest`, both output paths have ordinary
119: ancestors and pass their own alias checks, while the extra input keeps
120: the alias spelling and compares unequal. `-Force` would then remove the
121: file that extra input names. This tool cannot establish physical identity
122: there, so an extra input reached through a directory link is REFUSED
123: rather than compared.
124: 
125: **Validation completes before anything is removed.** The same round found
126: that a draft deleting a pre-existing sidecar under `-Force` inside the
127: lexical block would delete it BEFORE the alias block refuses an aliased
128: mirror path, so a build that was going to be rejected had already
129: destroyed a file. The removal moves after every check.
130: 
131: **A root and a resolution failure are different answers.** The helper
132: returns one of three kinds, `ok`, `root` or `error`. A draft returned the
133: same null for both, so both callers announced "filesystem root".
134: Measured 2026-09-05: Windows PowerShell 5.1's provider accepted a
135: 278-character absolute path that `GetFullPath` refused with
136: `PathTooLongException`, while PowerShell 7 accepted it, so on 5.1 a
137: long-path build would have reported a filesystem root and never reached
138: the path-budget refusal that names the real problem.
139: 
140: **A root is detected through the framework, not through the leaf.**
141: Measured 2026-09-05: `Split-Path 'C:\' -Leaf` returns `C:\`, not `C:`, so
142: a regex on the leaf never fires for a drive root; and
143: `Split-Path '\\server\share\' -Leaf` returns `share` with parent
144: `\\server`, which rejoined would name a DIFFERENT SHARE. The two hosts
145: disagree on the UNC case. So `GetPathRoot` decides, and the suffix is
146: appended to the full path rather than rejoined to a parent.
147: 
148: **Create-new bounds the final component only.** It stops an overwrite and
149: a link substituted at that name. It cannot defend a DIRECTORY component:
150: an ancestor replaced by a junction before the open redirects creation
151: into that junction's target, and no open flag prevents it. The alias
152: block is what covers ancestors. The design states that division rather
153: than letting create-new appear to cover both.
154: 
155: **D2b. The explanation states only what the comparison measures, and the
156: reader enforces every bound it advertises.** Two rounds of review found
157: overstatements here, each one an instance of the very class this cycle
158: exists to remove. The corrected behaviour is part of this design rather
159: than a detail of it:
160: 
161: - Manifest membership is not file existence. A deletion-only entry is
162:   omitted from the manifest by `Get-ManifestSubject`
163:   (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:460-463`), and a clean tracked file that
164:   becomes dirty ENTERS it. So the groups are named `entered manifest
165:   coverage` and `left manifest coverage`, never `appeared` and
166:   `vanished`.
167: - No content difference does not prove the status listing changed. A
168:   replaced sidecar produces the same empty result. The fallback says the
169:   advisory manifest did not identify the cause, and stops there.
170: - A malformed record is reported, never silently dropped. That requires
171:   CHECKING THE GRAMMAR: a draft that only rejected a missing separator
172:   accepted `bad.txt not-a-hash` as an ordinary record, so a truncated
173:   digest became a reported difference instead of an admission of
174:   incompleteness. The hash field is validated, and duplicates and
175:   over-long records are counted.
176: - An EMPTY line is a malformed record, with no special case. A draft
177:   skipped every empty string as "the trailing split artifact", which
178:   silently dropped leading and interior empty records too. Records are
179:   read with a `StringReader`, which returns nothing at all for a file
180:   ending in a newline, so the artifact does not exist to special-case.
181:   The fix removes the condition rather than refining it.
182: - TRUNCATION IS ITS OWN STATE, never a malformed count. A draft capped
183:   the record loop by incrementing the malformed counter once and
184:   abandoning the remainder, so 200,003 malformed records reported
185:   200,001 and a resource limit was reported as a grammar diagnosis. The
186:   reader reports truncation, and says the remainder was not examined.
187: - The cap applies DURING extraction. A `-split` materializes every line
188:   of a file whose size is someone else's choice before any later cap can
189:   matter, so the bound has to live in the reader that produces the
190:   records.
191: - The size limit bounds THE READ. A draft measured the file with
192:   `Get-Item` and then read it with a separate `ReadAllBytes`, which
193:   bounds nothing, and `Get-Item`'s failure is non-terminating in a script
194:   that never sets `$ErrorActionPreference`, so an unreadable file left
195:   the test unmade and carried on. One handle now measures and reads. The
196:   record count and record length are bounded too, because a byte limit
197:   alone does not bound the working set a split produces.
198: - Untrusted text is rendered by UNICODE CATEGORY, not by a numeric
199:   range. `[int]$ch -lt 32` passes the C1 controls U+0085 and U+009B,
200:   which are terminal escape introducers, and passes U+2028 and the
201:   bidirectional override U+202E. Control, Format, LineSeparator,
202:   ParagraphSeparator and Surrogate cover them by name, and the sidecar
203:   path and any exception message go through the same renderer.
204: - The reader does not resolve links, and the design says so rather than
205:   implying otherwise. Worst case it prints misleading names from an
206:   unrelated file, which is why every emitted comparison is introduced as
207:   unauthenticated and advisory, and why the refusal never depends on it.
208: 
209: **D3. The quiet period ships in the skill.** A contract region states it,
210: covering build through wrapper exit, so it travels with the plugin.
211: 
212: The region states the operational rule AND its measurement limits,
213: because a contract region that claims more than its mechanism delivers is
214: the defect class this whole cycle is about. Three limits, all found by
215: cross-vendor review of the first draft:
216: 
217: - The check samples endpoints. A change made and reverted inside the
218:   round is not detected, and no before-and-after check could detect it
219:   (`tools/dispatch-round.ps1:305-307` says so already).
220: - A tracked file that git reports CLEAN is covered by neither
221:   fingerprint, so a raw-byte change surviving the clean filter is not
222:   covered (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:726-731`).
223: - Only the THIRD comparison costs a reviewer round. The preparation check
224:   and the wrapper's pre-client check both run before the client is
225:   invoked, so tripping either wastes no quota. The first draft said a
226:   round that trips the rule always spends its quota for nothing, which is
227:   true only of the post-client check.
228: 
229: **D4. The build is ordered last.** `references/preflight-mirror.md` gains
230: a timing rule: every act that writes inside the reviewed repository
231: finishes before the mirror is built.
232: 
233: ## Out of scope
234: 
235: - Any change to the digest's coverage (see D1).
236: - Item 91's cost question (the digest hashing a linked reference checkout
237:   repeatedly). Unrelated, already filed.
238: - Item 76's question about `.claude/skills` being materialised into the
239:   mirror. Unrelated, already filed.
240: 
241: ## Success criteria
242: 
243: - An ignored-file edit between build and verify produces a refusal that
244:   NAMES the file, on both PowerShell hosts.
245: - Deleting the advisory manifest, and replacing it with invalid bytes,
246:   each leave the exit code unchanged. Two cases, not a universal proof:
247:   they establish those two states and the general property rests on the
248:   call site, where the explanation runs after the refusal is decided and
249:   its output is never read.
250: - A case-only rename is reported rather than collapsed.
251: - A record with a non-hash digest field is counted as unreadable, and a
252:   trailing newline is not.
253: - A C1 control and a bidirectional override in an advisory name reach the
254:   operator rendered, never raw.
255: - A file past the reader's limit is refused by the read itself.
256: - A destination collision with the override, with a declared extra input,
257:   or with a link ancestor is refused BEFORE anything is removed, proven
258:   by a test asserting the pre-existing file survives a refused build.
259: - A drive root and a UNC root both yield no sidecar, on both hosts.
260: - `test_contract_coverage.py` reports the new region locked.
261: - All six CI tiers and both PowerShell hosts pass.

1: ---
2: name: multi-model-verify
3: description: Use when planning a reference port, an API-sensitive module, or any change risky enough to need cross-model verification before implementation, or when an implemented diff must be checked against its plan and reference before merge.
4: ---
5: 
6: # Multi-Model Verify
7: 
8: ## Controller host gate
9: 
10: This controller requires **Claude Code**: plugin-root substitution, background notifications, hooks, and reviewer agents are host contracts. Verify the available tools before preflight.
11: 
12: **Codex-to-Codex is same-vendor** and cannot satisfy this cross-vendor gate. No Codex controller adapter is shipped. In Codex, prepare the brief or inspect the protocol, report the review gate unavailable in this host, and identify the Claude controller handoff. Continue independent authorized work. Never invent tools, emit a FULL attestation, or silently downgrade; `references/fallbacks.md` governs consent. The remaining procedure is Claude-specific.
13: 
14: ## Overview
15: 
16: The session and cross-vendor reviewer are equal-weight advisors: verify and
17: refute claims with source evidence. The primary codex CLI lane's model and
18: evidence contracts are in references/model-prompting-notes.md. A backup lane
19: substitutes only through references/fallbacks.md's consent gate, preserving
20: `Verification status: FULL` when its cross-vendor checks pass. Read
21: references/backup-lane.md before any backup round.
22: Mode diff's debate is preceded by a REQUIRED whole-branch review from
23: the fable-reviewer seat (agents/fable-reviewer.md), and the user may
24: convene multi-reviewer PANELS (references/panels.md) in either mode.
25: 
26: **REQUIRED READING before the first round:** references/debate-protocol.md.
27: 
28: Claude's review-companion hook can invoke mode `diff` alongside superpowers
29: requesting-code-review. Explicit project review gates remain applicable.
30: 
31: ## When to use
32: 
33: - **Mode `plan`** — after superpowers brainstorming proposes approaches for a
34:   port or API-sensitive module, before the implementation plan is written.
35: - **Mode `diff`** — after implementation, alongside superpowers
36:   requesting-code-review (the review-companion hook injects this
37:   automatically with the matching base/head SHAs).
38: - NOT for plain bugfixes, docs or release chores, GUI tweaks, or lint sweeps.
39: 
40: ## Preflight (both modes)
41: 
42: 1. `codex --version` must succeed, and `codex login status` must report
43:    `Logged in using ChatGPT` — exit 0 alone also passes an API-key login,
44:    which rides different billing. On failure follow references/fallbacks.md
45:    (the consent gate may offer the cross-vendor backup lane —
46:    references/backup-lane.md — before any single-vendor degraded mode;
47:    never silently skip cross-vendor review).
48: 2. For port work: the reference source must exist under `References/`
49:    (quote paths — some contain spaces, e.g. `References/M+ Timer`). Missing
50:    → **HARD STOP before any debate round runs**: ask the user for the path
51:    and go no further — do not run a codex exchange about the absence, do
52:    not debate from memory, do not start a degraded mode. If the folder
53:    holds version subdirectories (e.g. a `v1.4/` next to a `v1.1_old/`), use
54:    the newest unless the user names one, and cite the versioned path:
55:    `References/<name>/<version>/<file>:<line>`.
56:    For non-port work there is no reference folder — claims ground in the
57:    project's own source, specs, or upstream docs instead, same strike rule.
58: 3. The reviewed repo must carry no AGENTS.md, no `.agents/` entries, and no
59:    `.kimi-code/` entries: codex auto-ingests AGENTS.md as instructions, and
60:    it advertises repo-level `.agents/skills/*/SKILL.md` to the model, which
61:    read a planted one as its FIRST action (both probed 2026-07-24: the
62:    planted AGENTS.md controlled the reviewer's reply; the planted skill
63:    entered its context; see model-prompting-notes.md) — back-channels into
64:    the auditor that break independence. kimi-code, the backup lane's
65:    client, documents `.kimi-code/skills/` as a project-level discovery
66:    root too (references/backup-lane.md), so the sweep covers it
67:    identically. That 2026-07-31 comparison was CONFOUNDED: `Skill` was
68:    denied in both arms, so the comparison did not isolate the flag.
69:    Re-probed 2026-08-03, the flag DOES suppress the home root while its
70:    target stays empty (references/backup-lane.md). This enumeration is
71:    the PRIMARY control for the reviewed tree's skills and agents, not
72:    defence in depth. Enumerate the whole
73:    tree in one listing — `git ls-files --cached --others '*AGENTS.md'
74:    '.agents/*' '.kimi-code/*'` — which covers tracked, untracked, AND
75:    ignored files: `--others` without `--exclude-standard` lists ignored
76:    paths, re-verified 2026-07-28, and `.git` itself is never listed.
77:    <!-- contract:start id=enumeration-depth-asymmetry -->
78:    The two pathspecs do not reach equally far. `*AGENTS.md` carries a
79:    leading star, so it lists a nested AGENTS.md at any depth. `.agents/*`
80:    is anchored at the repo ROOT, so a nested `sub/.agents/skills/x/` is
81:    NOT listed. Measured 2026-07-28 on codex-cli 0.144.1: the harness
82:    advertises a ROOT `.agents/skills` entry and does not advertise a
83:    nested one, so the asymmetry is not reachable today, and the client
84:    probe below reads what was loaded rather than where it might live.
85:    Widen the pathspec if that ever changes.
86:    <!-- contract:end -->
87:    <!-- contract:start id=back-channel-auto-mirror -->
88:    If present: BUILD THE MIRROR AND REPORT. Do NOT ask first - every
89:    deletion happens in a file COPY, and the remediation commit runs with
90:    repository hooks suppressed, so nothing in the reviewed tree executes
91:    and there is no destructive act to consent to. What was found is
92:    still EVIDENCE and still goes in the debate record with its paths,
93:    and the post-mirror re-enumeration must still come back empty before
94:    any round dispatches. A mirror that cannot be built - path budget
95:    blown, scratch unavailable, hooks not suppressible - is BLOCKED,
96:    never a fallback to dispatching over the real tree.
97:    <!-- contract:end -->
98:    Run
99:    `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1 -RepoRoot <repo> -MirrorPath <scratch>`
100:    at a SHORT `<scratch>` directly under the temp directory, never inside
101:    the session scratchpad, to build the **review mirror**, remediate the
102:    offending entries there, re-run the enumeration and the client probe
103:    below against it, and print the record block; empty enumeration
104:    output is the evidence. Whether the removal needs a commit branches on
105:    tracked-ness; references/backup-lane.md states that branch and the
106:    hook behaviour that comes with it. Full construction detail and the
107:    mirror's identity fields are in references/preflight-mirror.md.
108: 
109:    **The reviewer's own machine is the second half of this check, and the
110:    enumeration above cannot see it.** Run
111:    `tools/codex-context-probe.ps1 -WorkDir <dispatch cwd> -SuppressSkills -OverrideOut <verified-override-file> -Json`
112:    before round 1, with a FRESH scratch path for the override file (the
113:    mirror script runs it for you and prints the same result). It renders
114:    the model-visible prompt with `codex debug prompt-input`, which spends
115:    no tokens, sorts every ADVERTISED SKILL by the directory it came from,
116:    and checks the named instruction and feature blocks around them:
117:    anything inside the reviewed tree STOPS and is remediated in the
118:    mirror, anything from the codex plugin cache must be empty, and the
119:    global `AGENTS.md` plus any surviving home-scoped skill is recorded in
120:    the debate record with its path.
121:    <!-- contract:start id=client-context-probe -->
122:    A probe that cannot be taken, that exits non-zero, that returns output
123:    this parser cannot read, or that finds a named block missing is a
124:    transport failure and stops the round. It is never read as a clean
125:    result: an unmade measurement and a clean one must never look alike.
126:    <!-- contract:end -->
127: 
128:    <!-- contract:start id=plugin-cache-reclassified -->
129:    The user's codex plugin cache is NOT a harmless environment note.
130:    Measured 2026-07-28 on codex-cli 0.144.1, it delivered 31 skills into
131:    the reviewer's context, one of whose descriptions alone instructs the
132:    model to invoke a skill before answering anything; a reviewer in
133:    another session adopted it and answered without opening the plan.
134:    `--disable plugins --disable apps` removes it, and the probe's second
135:    pass is what proves the removal happened.
136:    <!-- contract:end -->
137: 
138:    <!-- contract:start id=client-probe-scope-limit -->
139:    State what a clean probe means, and never more. It means exactly this:
140:    no skill is advertised, no plugin or apps block is present, and no
141:    instruction source sits inside the reviewed tree. Three things it does
142:    NOT mean. The global `AGENTS.md` above survives a clean probe and is
143:    still instructing the reviewer; the probe records it rather than
144:    removing it. It says nothing about the TOOL surface, which is not in
145:    the prompt and is measured separately by the tool-surface probe in
146:    references/model-prompting-notes.md, where a clean result is a
147:    mitigation, never proof of removal. And full flag parity with the
148:    dispatch cannot be REQUESTED: `prompt-input` rejects `--sandbox` and
149:    `-m`, so whether either changes rendered content is UNVERIFIED. Do not
150:    call a passing probe full reviewer isolation.
151:    <!-- contract:end -->
152: 
153: ## Mode plan
154: 
155: 1. Draft the session position: chosen approach, port-fidelity claims, and the
156:    API/behavior risk register. Every reference claim cites a
157:    `References/<name>/<file>:<line>` actually read this session — anchor
158:    EVERY file with its full path the first time you cite it, manifests and
159:    secondary files included (shorthand only once that file is anchored);
160:    every API claim cites authoritative local docs (WoW projects:
161:    `.wow-api-reference/` or a dated in-game probe result) — never memory.
162:    The position has no separate artifact — it IS the claims section of the
163:    step-2 brief.
164: <!-- call:codex-fresh -->
165: 2. Compose the reviewer's debate brief per references/model-prompting-notes.md, write
166:    it to a scratchpad file, then write this wrapper body to `<wrapper-file>` — as
167:    a FILE, never from a here-string, whose terminator cannot survive this block's
168:    indentation — and prepare it with the tool. Never run it inline: a foreground
169:    call owns the session, so nobody can see the round or talk to the agent while
170:    it runs. The ceiling is not a kill; visibility is the reason.
171: 
172:    The wrapper body is today's block with the exit scaffolding added:
173: 
174:    <!-- wrapper:codex-fresh -->
175:    ```powershell
176:    $code = 1
177:    $priorOutputEncoding = $OutputEncoding
178:    try {
179:    $OutputEncoding = New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false)
180:    $brief = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllText("<brief-file>", (New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false, $true)))
181:    $bytes = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllBytes("<verified-override-file>")
182:    $seen = ([System.BitConverter]::ToString(([System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::Create()).ComputeHash($bytes)) -replace '-', '').ToLower()
183:    if ($seen -cne "<override-sha256>") { throw "the override file changed after the probe verified it" }
184:    $override = (New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false, $true)).GetString($bytes)
185:    $brief | codex exec --sandbox read-only --disable plugins --disable apps --disable memories -c mcp_servers.node_repl.enabled=false -c $override -m <canonical-model-id> -c model_reasoning_effort=<canonical-effort> --output-last-message $PSScriptRoot/reply - > $PSScriptRoot/transcript 2>&1
186:    $code = $LASTEXITCODE
187:    } catch { $code = 1 } finally { $OutputEncoding = $priorOutputEncoding }
188:    [System.IO.File]::WriteAllText("$PSScriptRoot/exit", "$code")
189:    exit $code
190:    ```
191: 
192:    `$PSScriptRoot` is the dispatch directory, because the tool installs the
193:    body into it as `body.ps1` and runs it there as a child process. That
194:    removes the need to pass a path in and removes one more thing a copy
195:    can get wrong.
196: 
197:    Run `-Prepare`, naming `-DispatchHost` explicitly as the caller's own
198:    host — the bare token `pwsh` or `powershell`, DERIVED from
199:    `(Get-Process -Id $PID).Path` rather than guessed; the tool refuses a
200:    full path — and passing `-WorkdirEvidence` with the resolved
201:    mirror path — the codex lane's transcript header carries a `workdir:`
202:    line naming it, confirmed on a real round:
203: 
204:    ```powershell
205:    & (Get-Process -Id $PID).Path -NoProfile -File ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/tools/dispatch-round.ps1 -Prepare -DispatchDir <dispatch-dir> -WrapperBody <wrapper-file> -ReceiptPath <receipt-file> -Round <label> -WorkingDirectory <mirror-path> -RepoRoot <repo-root> -SourceHead <source-head> -MirrorHead <mirror-head> -SourceStatusSha256 <source-status-sha256> -MirrorStateSha256 <mirror-state-sha256> -ExpectedMirrorPath <mirror-path> -DispatchHost <dispatch-host> -PriorStateFile <prior-state-file> -WorkdirEvidence <mirror-path> -Json
206:    ```
207: 
208:    `<receipt-file>` is a FRESH path, alongside the fresh reply and
209:    transcript paths already required; `-Prepare` refuses one that
210:    exists. `<label>` names the lane and the round, as in `Astra R1`.
211:    `-Prepare` prints `command` and `taskName`: dispatch it as a harness background command, running `command` exactly as printed, under the `taskName` the tool printed, and STOP — but never END THE TURN with the round unfinished (references/model-prompting-notes.md's round-dispatch-operation).
212: 
213:    On the completion notification for that exact task, read the harness output file: the exit code of that exact task is the result, never re-read the dispatch directory for a verdict — **0 means `reply-present` and nothing else; 2 is a parameter-binding failure or an internal execution error; 1 is every other state, named on the wrapper's last stdout line.** The states -Classify computes are references/model-prompting-notes.md's round-dispatch-states.
214: 
215:    Both encoding lines are load-bearing on Windows PowerShell 5.1
216:    (references/model-prompting-notes.md).
217: 
218:    <!-- contract:start id=verified-override-dispatch -->
219:    The `-c` value MUST be the file the probe wrote with `-OverrideOut`, on
220:    round 1 and on every resume, read as raw bytes whose hash is checked
221:    against the probe's report before use. The two feature flags alone
222:    still leave the user's own skills directory and codex's built-in skills
223:    advertised, which was 29 of the original 60 when this was measured;
224:    only the generated override removes those, and only the probe's second
225:    pass proves it did. A dispatch that omits the override, or carries a
226:    value the probe did not verify, is a transport failure, because the
227:    measurement then describes a configuration the reviewer never received.
228:    <!-- contract:end -->
229: 
230:    `<canonical-model-id>` and `<canonical-effort>` are the two declarations
231:    in references/model-prompting-notes.md — read the literal values from
232:    there, never from memory (the reviewer swaps by editing that one file,
233:    and a remembered id silently defeats the swap). Apply that file's env
234:    hygiene to the invocation.
235: 
236:    Backup lane: same protocol, transport and per-round evidence per references/backup-lane.md.
237: 
238:    Panels: any reviewer-lane combination per references/panels.md.
239: 
240:    From `<transcript-file>`: verify the effective route — the header's
241:    `model:`, `provider:`, and `reasoning effort:` lines against the
242:    canonical declarations, and the `sandbox:` line reads `read-only`, per
243:    model-prompting-notes.md; a mismatch is a transport failure
244:    (fallbacks.md), never a review result — and capture the `session id:`
245:    line. Read the reviewer's reply from `<reply-file>` — the transcript
246:    logs every file the reviewer reads and can run tens of KB, with the
247:    reply buried at the bottom (a reused path, which `-Prepare` refuses,
248:    would read exactly like success).
249:    Per-round evidence: bind the reply to the brief THIS side sent with
250:    `tools/read-codex-round-evidence.ps1` — `-Fresh` at round 1, `-Resume`
251:    after. `-PriorState` is captured immediately before EVERY dispatch —
252:    an inventory of the session root before round 1, then each round's
253:    own state — and is never inherited from the last clean round; a state
254:    with a missing field is refused, never assumed empty. Pass
255:    `-SealedPriorStateSha256` with the receipt's `priorStateSha256` on
256:    EVERY call — mandatory here though optional on the binder for other
257:    callers; `sealed: "not-checked"` is a transport failure, not a clean
258:    round. A verdict other
259:    than clean is class `brief-attribution` (fallbacks.md): the reply is
260:    discarded unread. A clean verdict is client-echo evidence — what the
261:    client recorded, never what any server received.
262: 
263: <!-- call:codex-resume -->
264: 3. Later rounds keep the reviewer's state by resuming that session — flags
265:    MUST precede the resume subcommand (flags after it are a usage error).
266:    Compose the rebuttal and launch the same wrapper shape as round 1,
267:    with the resumed call:
268: 
269:    <!-- wrapper:codex-resume -->
270:    ```powershell
271:    $code = 1
272:    $priorOutputEncoding = $OutputEncoding
273:    try {
274:    $OutputEncoding = New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false)
275:    $brief = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllText("<brief-file>", (New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false, $true)))
276:    $bytes = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllBytes("<verified-override-file>")
277:    $seen = ([System.BitConverter]::ToString(([System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::Create()).ComputeHash($bytes)) -replace '-', '').ToLower()
278:    if ($seen -cne "<override-sha256>") { throw "the override file changed after the probe verified it" }
279:    $override = (New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false, $true)).GetString($bytes)
280:    $brief | codex exec --sandbox read-only --disable plugins --disable apps --disable memories -c mcp_servers.node_repl.enabled=false -c $override -m <canonical-model-id> -c model_reasoning_effort=<canonical-effort> --output-last-message $PSScriptRoot/reply resume <SESSION_ID> - > $PSScriptRoot/transcript 2>&1
281:    $code = $LASTEXITCODE
282:    } catch { $code = 1 } finally { $OutputEncoding = $priorOutputEncoding }
283:    [System.IO.File]::WriteAllText("$PSScriptRoot/exit", "$code")
284:    exit $code
285:    ```
286: 
287:    Run `-Prepare` the same way as round 1, with `-DispatchHost` and
288:    `-WorkdirEvidence` set the same way. A resumed round needs the
289:    mirror at the path its identity was recorded at: if the tree had to be
290:    rebuilt mid-debate, rebuild it at the SAME path
291:    with `-Force` and re-record its identity fields, because a mirror at a new
292:    path makes the binder refuse the resumed slice on `cwd` and the round is
293:    lost. If it cannot be rebuilt there, dispatch FRESH rather than resume.
294: 
295:    ```powershell
296:    & (Get-Process -Id $PID).Path -NoProfile -File ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/tools/dispatch-round.ps1 -Prepare -DispatchDir <dispatch-dir> -WrapperBody <wrapper-file> -ReceiptPath <receipt-file> -Round <label> -WorkingDirectory <mirror-path> -RepoRoot <repo-root> -SourceHead <source-head> -MirrorHead <mirror-head> -SourceStatusSha256 <source-status-sha256> -MirrorStateSha256 <mirror-state-sha256> -ExpectedMirrorPath <mirror-path> -DispatchHost <dispatch-host> -PriorStateFile <prior-state-file> -WorkdirEvidence <mirror-path> -Json
297:    ```
298: 
299:    `-Prepare` prints `command` and `taskName`: dispatch it as a harness background command, running `command` exactly as printed, under the `taskName` the tool printed, and STOP — but never END THE TURN with the round unfinished (references/model-prompting-notes.md's round-dispatch-operation).
300: 
301:    On the completion notification for that exact task, read the harness output file: the exit code of that exact task is the result, never re-read the dispatch directory for a verdict — **0 means `reply-present` and nothing else; 2 is a parameter-binding failure or an internal execution error; 1 is every other state, named on the wrapper's last stdout line.** The states -Classify computes are references/model-prompting-notes.md's round-dispatch-states.
302: 
303:    Bind the reply the same way as round 1, with `-Resume` in place of
304:    `-Fresh`, and pass `-SealedPriorStateSha256` with THIS round's
305:    `priorStateSha256`, captured immediately before this dispatch.
306: 
307:    The preamble repeats in full every round. Rounds are separate shell
308:    invocations, so a `$override` set in round 1 does not exist in round 3,
309:    and one verification does not cover a file that can change between
310:    rounds.
311: 
312:    Each resume's header must echo the resumed `session id:` and the same
313:    effective route — the round-1 check repeated, `sandbox:` included.
314:    Sandbox mode has NO continuity across resumes, so the flag is re-pinned
315:    on every one; references/model-prompting-notes.md carries the probe that
316:    measured it and what a dropped flag does.
317: 
318: 4. Iterate per debate-protocol.md until convergence or the round cap, then
319:    escalate any unresolved points to the user with both positions stated.
320: 5. Freeze the converged plan per references/frozen-plan-format.md under the
321:    project's superpowers plans dir (KitnEssentials:
322:    `dev/docs/superpowers/plans/`; other projects: the superpowers default
323:    `docs/superpowers/plans/`).
324: 
325: ## Mode diff
326: 
327: Same transport and protocol. First read the frozen plan's debate record and
328: its **Verification status** field.
329: 
330: Required before round 1: the agents/fable-reviewer.md whole-branch review runs on the same range, its raw reply is retained as a range-bound artifact, and the round-1 brief cites that artifact with the session's per-finding adjudications.
331: The session adjudicates each review finding with evidence — accept,
332: refute, or ESCALATE into the debate — before any reviewer lane sees
333: them; the review is the debate's required input, never its verdict,
334: and the debate record names the artifact path.
335: 
336: Then the brief carries the frozen plan
337: path, the base/head SHAs superpowers code review used, and the
338: `git diff base..head` output. Both sides check **spec fidelity** (drift from
339: the frozen plan — the implementer makes zero judgment calls, so any drift is
340: a finding, with one carve-out: envelope-designated escalation-lane DECISIONS
341: are adjudicated against their frozen envelope per
342: references/frozen-plan-format.md, and only envelope overruns are drift) and,
343: for port work, **port fidelity** (drift from the reference
344: source), ending PASS / FIX / ESCALATE.
345: 
346: Backup lane: same protocol, transport and per-round evidence per references/backup-lane.md.
347: 
348: Panels: any reviewer-lane combination per references/panels.md.
349: 
350: A FIX this session applies itself goes through the **application
351: checkpoint** (references/application-checkpoint.md) before the first edit.
352: The fixed range is then re-reviewed, and only the post-re-review terminal
353: PASS is attested — with the emitter's `-CheckpointFile` binding the
354: checkpoint; a FIX verdict whose fixes are still unapplied is never the
355: attested verdict.
356: 
357: **Degraded-plan poisoning rule:** a plan whose Verification status is
358: DEGRADED cannot produce an ordinary diff PASS. Mode diff must first reopen
359: and cross-verify the plan's claims and approved deviations (the
360: retrospective pass the plan never got), and only then verify the
361: implementation against it — confirming a diff matches an unsound plan
362: verifies nothing. If cross-vendor verification is STILL unavailable, the
363: only terminal state is `ESCALATE — CROSS-VENDOR GATE UNSATISFIED`: merging
364: then requires the user's explicitly recorded risk acceptance, never a PASS.
365: 
366: ## Finish line
367: 
368: Before it: the session's final-adjudication step (debate-protocol.md) —
369: verify the last round's findings against the repo and issue the terminal
370: verdict; a reviewer's PASS/FIX is never terminal by itself.
371: 
372: A PASS is terminal only for the exact head it was issued on. If you apply
373: anything the reviewer raised — including observations it labelled
374: non-blocking — the head moves and the verdict no longer covers it. Either
375: leave them for a follow-up branch, or run one confirming round.
376: 
377: **Mode diff only — record the verdict mechanically.** After the terminal
378: verdict, run the attestation emitter from this plugin's checkout:
379: 
380: ```powershell
381: powershell -NoProfile -File <plugin-root>/tools/write-attestation.ps1 -RepoRoot <reviewed-repo> -BaseSha <base> -HeadSha <head> -Verdict <PASS|FIX|ESCALATE> -VerificationStatus <FULL|DEGRADED> -RouteNote "<effective route confirmed | the transport-failure class>" -Rounds <n> -Participants "<session-model> (session) / <reviewer-model> (reviewer)" [-CheckpointFile <application-checkpoint-artifact>]
382: ```
383: 
384: When an application checkpoint governed fix application, pass it via
385: `-CheckpointFile`; references/application-checkpoint.md states what that
386: binds and which head it is bound to.
387: 
388: It writes `.git/parallax/attestations/<head-sha>.json` inside the reviewed
389: repo — untracked by design, so recording the verdict cannot move HEAD out
390: from under its own SHA. The pre-push lane (`tools/verify-attestation.ps1`)
391: later warns when a `main` push has no matching attestation for the pushed
392: head (fast-forward: pushed sha == attested head; merge commit: parent1 ==
393: attested base, parent2 == attested head — a squash changes the sha and
394: correctly forces re-review).
395: 
396: End with one status line: participating models, rounds used, converged vs
397: escalated points, the verification status — `FULL`, or
398: `DEGRADED (<class>, authorized by user at round N)` per fallbacks.md — and
399: the route note: `effective route confirmed` when every round's header
400: matched the canonical declarations, else the transport-failure class that
401: fallbacks.md handled. The route-note grammar is lane-agnostic: for a backup-lane debate,
402: `effective route confirmed` means every round satisfied
403: references/backup-lane.md's per-round evidence rules against the backup
404: declarations; the evidence class is recorded in the debate record prose. The session emits this line itself; never delegate it
405: to a subagent.
406: 
407: ## Common mistakes
408: 
409: - Accepting the reviewer's claims about reference code without the cited lines —
410:   strike the claim per protocol; do not argue against it.
411: - Re-sending the full debate context each round instead of resuming the
412:   codex session.
413: - Running mode `diff` against different SHAs than the code review used.
414: - Treating convergence as failure — a sound plan converging in one round is
415:   the system working, not a skipped debate.

1: # parallax — Claude Code plugin (NOT a WoW addon)
2: 
3: This repo is developer tooling: a Claude Code plugin providing cross-model
4: verification (session ⇄ cross-vendor reviewer debates; the reviewer lane is
5: declared in the skill's model-prompting-notes.md) plus its eval harness. The WoW addon family conventions (dev loop, /reload, luacheck/busted,
6: 12.0 API rules, References/) do NOT apply here — only the git basics do (feature branches
7: for real work, lowercase imperative commits, no AI attribution).
8: 
9: ## Verification
10: - `python evals/tools/skill_lint.py skills/multi-model-verify --strict`
11: - `python evals/tools/skill_scanner.py skills`
12: - `python evals/tools/check_exact_line_oracles.py`
13: - `python evals/tools/run_trigger_evals.py`
14: - `python -m pytest evals -q`
15: - `python evals/tools/backlog_lint.py`
16: CI runs all six on every push (.github/workflows/skill-evals.yml), as tiers
17: 1, 1b, 1c, 2, 2b and 2c of the `skill-evals` job; tier 2d runs the same
18: governed-range test as the pre-push hook, on main pushes and pull requests.
19: 
20: A SECOND job, `powershell-hosts`, re-runs every PowerShell-facing test
21: module under BOTH Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7. A green suite
22: on one host proves ONE interpreter: 0.16.0 shipped a lane lock that did
23: not lock on PowerShell 7 at all, and the Linux job only caught it after
24: release. Locally the suite picks whichever host it finds first, so set
25: `$env:PARALLAX_PS_HOST` to test the other one.
26: 
27: Tier 1c is a mechanical sweep for one defect class, not a proof: it flags
28: the "discard blank lines, then assert exactly one survivor" idiom, which
29: accepts a reply the frozen contract must reject. Three hand sweeps each
30: missed an instance. Write new single-line parsers through
31: `accept_exactly_one_nonempty_line()` in `evals/tools/exact_line.py`.
32: 
33: Three suites are local-only and opt-in, so run them by hand when you touch
34: what they cover:
35: - skill/prompt changes -> `python evals/tools/run_behavioral_evals.py`
36:   (real headless runs, graded by the cross-vendor reviewer; `--head` tests
37:   the checkout instead of the installed cache; for small contract edits
38:   `--changed` runs only cases whose declared surface intersects the diff
39:   vs main, printing every skip by name).
40: - `tools/check-drift.ps1` changes -> run
41:   `evals/tools/drift_statemachine_tests.ps1` (or, to go through pytest:
42:   `$env:PARALLAX_STATEMACHINE = "1"; python -m pytest evals -q` — it is
43:   PowerShell, so no `VAR=1 cmd` prefix). Drives the real script through its
44:   whole state machine offline against stub CLIs. Slow: four scenarios
45:   re-run the full pytest suite inside a disposable worktree.
46: - backup-lane credential or lock changes -> set `PARALLAX_LANE_LIVE=1`
47:   and `PARALLAX_LANE_LIVE_HOME_{A,B,C}` to the three provisioned lane
48:   homes, then `python -m pytest evals -q`. Windows only. It drives the
49:   REAL kimi-code client. Unset, the module skips; once opted in, EVERY
50:   setup failure FAILS the gate instead of skipping it, because a skipped
51:   credential measurement is not a clean one.

32: skill_lint.py — validate an agent skill directory against the agentskills.io spec.
33: 
34: Usage:
35:     python3 skill_lint.py <skill-dir> [--strict] [--json]
36: 
37: Checks (spec: https://agentskills.io/specification, verified July 2026):
38:   * SKILL.md exists and has parseable YAML frontmatter
39:   * name: 1-64 chars, lowercase a-z/0-9/hyphens, no leading/trailing/consecutive
40:     hyphens, must equal the directory name
41:   * description: present, 1-1024 chars, plus triggering heuristics
42:   * compatibility: <= 500 chars if present
43:   * body: warn at 400 lines, 500+ lines is a warning (error with --strict);
44:     token budget - clean at or below BODY_TOKEN_BUDGET, WARNING above it,
45:     ERROR above BODY_TOKEN_CEILING (local delta, see the header)
46:   * relative file references in the body must exist on disk
47:   * forward-slash paths only (backslash paths are an error)
48:   * TODO/FIXME-style markers are warnings
49:   * text-only skills (no scripts/ AND no references/) are warned — the
50:     awesome-llm-apps quality bar expects bundled tools and references
51: 
52: Exit codes: 0 = no errors (warnings allowed), 1 = errors found, 2 = usage error.
53: A body over BODY_TOKEN_CEILING is an ERROR, so it exits 1 - that is the
54: point of the local delta, and it is the one way a token count can fail this
55: tool rather than merely print at it.
56: Python 3 stdlib only — no third-party dependencies (frontmatter parsing is
57: hand-rolled for the flat fields the spec defines; pyyaml is NOT required).
58: """

66: NAME_RE = re.compile(r"^[a-z0-9]+(?:-[a-z0-9]+)*$")
67: MAX_NAME = 64
68: MAX_DESC = 1024
69: MAX_COMPAT = 500
70: BODY_WARN_LINES = 400
71: BODY_MAX_LINES = 500
72: 
73: # GLOBAL LINTER POLICY, not a per-skill setting. Only one tracked SKILL.md
74: # exists in this repo today, but this file is a generic tool and these two
75: # numbers apply to every skill it is pointed at.
76: #
77: # Three MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE outcomes: clean at or below the soft target,
78: # WARNING above it up to the hard ceiling, ERROR above the ceiling. Never
79: # a warning and an error for the same body.
80: #
81: # BOTH NUMBERS ARE IN THIS TOOL'S OWN ESTIMATE, which is len(body) // 4
82: # and which the output calls "roughly". They are not tokenizer counts and
83: # they never were; swapping in a real tokenizer would move every number
84: # here without changing what the gate does. Read "5227" below as "5227 by
85: # this estimate". The cross-vendor lane narrowed this in the 0.23.0 diff
86: # debate, where it had been written as measured rather than estimated.
87: #
88: # 0.23.0 rebased both from a MEASURED body rather than a guessed one.
89: # Upstream shipped a 5000 warning that nothing enforced; it had sat over
90: # that number for several releases (backlog item 19). The relocations in
91: # that release brought multi-model-verify's body to 5069, and its
92: # mandatory UTF-8 brief transport - two encoding lines that CANNOT be
93: # deduplicated, because separate rounds run in separate shells - put it at
94: # 5227. The soft target is set from that measured baseline; the ceiling
95: # preserves the 250-token warning band the debate agreed on.
96: #
97: # These do NOT rebase automatically. A future release that measures over
98: # the ceiling must either relocate text to a reference file or change
99: # these numbers deliberately, with the measurement and the reason recorded
100: # here. Neither remedy is "delete a sentence a review asked for".
101: #
102: # RAISED 2026-08-31 (backlog item 32, task 3). Measured with the header's
103: # own command (`t.split('---',2)[2]`, `len(body)//4`) after routing both
104: # codex dispatch sites through tools/dispatch-round.ps1: 6225. The
105: # tool-based design was expected to shrink the body — it replaces five
106: # copied dispatch snippets with one tool the skill only calls — but each
107: # call site's per-site test (test_each_codex_call_is_launched_through_the_tool)
108: # requires the FULL wrapper body, the launch command, the poll command,
109: # and the whole four-clause exit-code sentence to appear inside that
110: # site's own section, so the fresh and resumed dispatches each carry their
111: # own copy of that shape; a global count would have let one site stay
112: # foreground undetected (round 6's finding), so the duplication is the
113: # point, not slack to trim. The resume section was already condensed to
114: # the minimum that still satisfies its own per-site literals before this
115: # number was accepted.

117: # The tool was named tools/dispatch-detached.ps1 when that 6225 was
118: # measured; the completion-coupled cycle renamed it to dispatch-round.ps1
119: # because it no longer detaches. The path above is updated so it resolves,

124: # and nothing else; the first attempt raised the BUDGET to 6250 as well,

129: # IS over it, deliberately: the measurement above is a figure bound to task
130: # 3's commit, not a promise about the file's size today, and the warning is
131: # the honest signal that the body has grown.
132: BODY_TOKEN_BUDGET = 5250
133: BODY_TOKEN_CEILING = 6500
134: 
135: KNOWN_KEYS = {"name", "description", "license", "compatibility", "metadata", "allowed-tools"}
136: 
137: WHEN_SIGNALS = re.compile(
138:     r"\b(use (this skill )?when|use (it |this )?for|when the user|when working"
139:     r"|use to|triggers? (on|when)|applies (when|to)|for tasks)\b",
140:     re.IGNORECASE,
141: )
142: FIRST_PERSON = re.compile(r"\b(I|I'll|I'm|I've|me|my|we|our|you should|you can)\b")
143: QUOTED_SPAN = re.compile(r"\"[^\"\n]*\"|'[^'\n]*'|“[^”\n]*”")
144: FENCED_BLOCK = re.compile(r"^```.*?^```", re.MULTILINE | re.DOTALL)
145: INLINE_CODE = re.compile(r"`[^`\n]+`")
146: # Unfilled template placeholders are multi-word angle-bracket spans in prose
147: # (e.g. "<what this skill does>"). Single-word forms like <path> are normal
148: # CLI-usage notation and HTML tags never contain multi-word lowercase text.
149: TEMPLATE_PLACEHOLDER = re.compile(r"<([a-z][a-z0-9'’,—-]*(?: [a-z0-9'’,—.…-]+)+[^>]*)>")
150: TODO_MARKER = re.compile(r"\b(TODO|FIXME|XXX|TBD)\b")
151: MD_LINK = re.compile(r"\]\(([^)\s#]+)\)")
152: BARE_PATH = re.compile(r"\b((?:scripts|references|assets|evals)/[\w.\-/]+)")
153: BACKSLASH_PATH = re.compile(r"(?:scripts|references|assets|evals)\\")
154: 
155: 
156: def unquote(value):
157:     value = value.strip()
158:     if len(value) >= 2 and value[0] == value[-1] and value[0] in ("'", '"'):
159:         return value[1:-1]
160:     return value
161: 
162: 
163: def parse_frontmatter(text):
164:     """Minimal YAML parser for the flat frontmatter the spec defines.
165: 
166:     Supports: `key: value`, quoted values, block scalars (| and >), one-level
167:     nested maps (for `metadata:`), and inline flow maps (`{a: b, c: d}`).
168:     Returns (data, body, error). data is None on a parse error.
169:     """
170:     lines = text.splitlines()
171:     if not lines or lines[0].strip() != "---":
172:         return None, text, "SKILL.md must begin with a '---' YAML frontmatter block on line 1"
173:     end = None
174:     for i in range(1, len(lines)):
175:         if lines[i].strip() in ("---", "..."):
176:             end = i
177:             break
178:     if end is None:
179:         return None, text, "frontmatter is never closed: add a '---' line after the YAML block"
180: 
181:     fm_lines = lines[1:end]
182:     body = "\n".join(lines[end + 1:])
183:     data = {}
184:     i = 0
185:     while i < len(fm_lines):
186:         raw = fm_lines[i]
187:         stripped = raw.strip()
188:         if not stripped or stripped.startswith("#"):
189:             i += 1
190:             continue
191:         if raw[:1] in (" ", "\t"):
192:             return None, body, (
193:                 "unexpected indentation at frontmatter line %d (%r): top-level keys "
194:                 "must start at column 0" % (i + 2, stripped)
195:             )
196:         if ":" not in raw:
197:             return None, body, "cannot parse frontmatter line %d: %r (expected 'key: value')" % (i + 2, stripped)
198:         key, _, val = raw.partition(":")
199:         key = key.strip()
200:         val = val.strip()
201: 
202:         if val in ("|", ">", "|-", ">-", "|+", ">+"):  # block scalar
203:             block = []
204:             i += 1
205:             while i < len(fm_lines) and (fm_lines[i][:1] in (" ", "\t") or not fm_lines[i].strip()):
206:                 block.append(fm_lines[i].strip())
207:                 i += 1
208:             joiner = " " if val.startswith(">") else "\n"
209:             data[key] = joiner.join(b for b in block if b)
210:             continue
211: 
212:         if val == "":  # nested one-level map (e.g. metadata:)
213:             nested = {}
214:             i += 1
215:             while i < len(fm_lines) and (fm_lines[i][:1] in (" ", "\t") or not fm_lines[i].strip()):
216:                 sub = fm_lines[i].strip()
217:                 if sub and not sub.startswith("#"):
218:                     if ":" not in sub:
219:                         return None, body, (
220:                             "cannot parse nested entry under %r at frontmatter line %d: %r" % (key, i + 2, sub)
221:                         )
222:                     k2, _, v2 = sub.partition(":")
223:                     nested[k2.strip()] = unquote(v2)
224:                 i += 1
225:             data[key] = nested
226:             continue
227: 
228:         if val.startswith("{") and val.endswith("}"):  # inline flow map
229:             nested = {}
230:             inner = val[1:-1].strip()
231:             if inner:
232:                 for part in inner.split(","):
233:                     if ":" not in part:
234:                         return None, body, "cannot parse inline map for %r: %r" % (key, part.strip())
235:                     k2, _, v2 = part.partition(":")
236:                     nested[k2.strip()] = unquote(v2)
237:             data[key] = nested
238:             i += 1
239:             continue
240: 
241:         data[key] = unquote(val)
242:         i += 1
243:     return data, body, None
244: 
245: 
246: def suggest_name(name):
247:     s = str(name).strip().lower()
248:     s = re.sub(r"[_\s]+", "-", s)
249:     s = re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9-]", "", s)
250:     s = re.sub(r"-{2,}", "-", s).strip("-")
251:     return s[:MAX_NAME] or "my-skill"
252: 
253: 
254: def check_name(name, dirname, errors):
255:     problems = []
256:     if not name:
257:         errors.append("frontmatter 'name' is missing or empty (required, 1-%d chars)" % MAX_NAME)
258:         return
259:     if len(name) > MAX_NAME:
260:         problems.append("longer than %d characters" % MAX_NAME)
261:     if re.search(r"[A-Z]", name):
262:         problems.append("uppercase not allowed")
263:     if re.search(r"[_\s]", name):
264:         problems.append("underscores/spaces not allowed")
265:     if re.search(r"[^a-zA-Z0-9\-_\s]", name):
266:         problems.append("only a-z, 0-9 and hyphens are allowed")
267:     if name.startswith("-") or name.endswith("-"):
268:         problems.append("must not start or end with a hyphen")
269:     if "--" in name:
270:         problems.append("consecutive hyphens not allowed")
271:     if problems or not NAME_RE.match(name):
272:         detail = "; ".join(problems) if problems else "does not match ^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$"
273:         errors.append("name %r invalid: %s; try %r" % (name, detail, suggest_name(name)))
274:         return
275:     if name != dirname:
276:         errors.append(
277:             "name %r must equal the skill directory name %r: rename the directory "
278:             "or set 'name: %s'" % (name, dirname, dirname)
279:         )
280: 
281: 
282: def check_description(desc, errors, warnings):
283:     if not desc:
284:         errors.append(
285:             "frontmatter 'description' is missing or empty (required, 1-%d chars); "
286:             "state WHAT the skill does and WHEN to use it, with trigger keywords" % MAX_DESC
287:         )
288:         return
289:     if len(desc) > MAX_DESC:
290:         errors.append(
291:             "description is %d characters (max %d): trim it — a few keyword-rich "
292:             "sentences outperform a paragraph dump" % (len(desc), MAX_DESC)
293:         )
294:     ph = TEMPLATE_PLACEHOLDER.search(desc)
295:     if ph:
296:         errors.append(
297:             "description contains an unfilled template placeholder %r: replace "
298:             "every angle-bracket placeholder with specifics before shipping — "
299:             "a scaffolded description must never pass as done" % ph.group(0)
300:         )
301:     if len(desc) < 50:
302:         warnings.append(
303:             "description is only %d characters — too thin to trigger reliably; add "
304:             "what the skill does plus 'Use when …' contexts with the keywords users "
305:             "actually type" % len(desc)
306:         )
307:     # Quoted trigger phrases ("evaluate my agent") legitimately contain
308:     # first-person words — strip quoted spans before the voice check.
309:     desc_unquoted = QUOTED_SPAN.sub(" ", desc)
310:     if FIRST_PERSON.search(desc_unquoted):
311:         warnings.append(
312:             "description uses first/second-person voice (%r) outside quoted "
313:             "trigger phrases: rewrite in third person, e.g. 'Extracts …. Use "
314:             "when the user …'"
315:             % FIRST_PERSON.search(desc_unquoted).group(0)
316:         )
317:     if not WHEN_SIGNALS.search(desc):
318:         warnings.append(
319:             "description never states when to use the skill: add an explicit "
320:             "'Use when …' clause with trigger phrases — the description is the "
321:             "only text agents see before activation"
322:         )
323: 
324: 
325: def check_body(body, skill_dir, strict, errors, warnings):
326:     body_lines = body.splitlines()
327:     n = len(body_lines)
328:     if n >= BODY_MAX_LINES:
329:         msg = (
330:             "SKILL.md body is %d lines (spec recommends < %d): move detail into "
331:             "references/ files with explicit load conditions" % (n, BODY_MAX_LINES)
332:         )
333:         (errors if strict else warnings).append(msg)
334:     elif n > BODY_WARN_LINES:
335:         warnings.append(
336:             "SKILL.md body is %d lines (warn threshold %d, spec limit %d): start "
337:             "moving reference material out now" % (n, BODY_WARN_LINES, BODY_MAX_LINES)
338:         )
339:     est_tokens = len(body) // 4
340:     if est_tokens > BODY_TOKEN_CEILING:
341:         # ERROR, and deliberately not also a warning: the two bands are
342:         # mutually exclusive so the output says one thing. A warning that
343:         # never fails is what let this number drift unowned for several
344:         # releases.
345:         errors.append(
346:             "SKILL.md body is roughly %d tokens, over the hard ceiling of %d. "
347:             "Two remedies: move text that is read only when a branch is taken "
348:             "into a references/ file, or raise the ceiling deliberately in "
349:             "skill_lint.py with the measurement and the reason recorded there. "
350:             "Deleting text a review asked for is not one of them."
351:             % (est_tokens, BODY_TOKEN_CEILING)
352:         )
353:     elif est_tokens > BODY_TOKEN_BUDGET:
354:         warnings.append(
355:             "SKILL.md body is roughly %d tokens (budget ~%d, hard ceiling %d): "
356:             "every token competes with the user's task once the skill activates"
357:             % (est_tokens, BODY_TOKEN_BUDGET, BODY_TOKEN_CEILING)
358:         )
359: 
360:     if BACKSLASH_PATH.search(body):
361:         errors.append(
362:             "backslash path found in SKILL.md (e.g. %r): the spec requires relative "
363:             "forward-slash paths" % BACKSLASH_PATH.search(body).group(0)
364:         )
365: 
366:     for marker in sorted(set(TODO_MARKER.findall(body))):
367:         warnings.append("unfinished-work marker %r found in SKILL.md body: resolve or remove it" % marker)
368: 
369:     # Fenced code blocks hold illustrative paths (example scanner reports,
370:     # template output) — only prose and links promise real files.
371:     prose = FENCED_BLOCK.sub("", body)
372: 
373:     # Unfilled scaffold placeholders in prose mean the skill is not done —
374:     # the whole point of "lint until clean" as a workflow gate. Inline code
375:     # spans are excluded (`<skill-dir>` CLI notation is legitimate).
376:     prose_no_code = INLINE_CODE.sub("", prose)
377:     for ph in sorted(set(m.group(0) for m in TEMPLATE_PLACEHOLDER.finditer(prose_no_code)))[:5]:
378:         errors.append(
379:             "unfilled template placeholder %s in SKILL.md body: replace it "
380:             "with real content before shipping" % ph
381:         )
382: 
383:     candidates = set(MD_LINK.findall(prose)) | set(BARE_PATH.findall(prose))
384:     for cand in sorted(candidates):
385:         cand = cand.rstrip(".,:;)`'\"")
386:         if not cand or cand.startswith(("http://", "https://", "mailto:", "#", "/")):
387:             continue
388:         if "\\" in cand:
389:             continue  # already reported as a backslash-path error
390:         if not os.path.exists(os.path.join(skill_dir, cand)):
391:             errors.append(
392:                 "SKILL.md references %r but no such file exists in the skill "
393:                 "directory: fix the path or add the file" % cand
394:             )
395: 
396: 
397: def lint(skill_dir, strict=False):
398:     errors, warnings = [], []
399:     skill_dir = os.path.abspath(skill_dir)
400:     dirname = os.path.basename(skill_dir)
401: 
402:     if not os.path.isdir(skill_dir):
403:         return ["%s is not a directory" % skill_dir], []
404: 
405:     skill_md = os.path.join(skill_dir, "SKILL.md")
406:     if not os.path.isfile(skill_md):
407:         errors.append("SKILL.md not found in %s (required at the skill root)" % skill_dir)
408:         return errors, warnings
409: 
410:     with open(skill_md, encoding="utf-8") as fh:
411:         text = fh.read()
412: 
413:     fm, body, perr = parse_frontmatter(text)
414:     if perr:
415:         errors.append("frontmatter parse error: %s" % perr)
416:         return errors, warnings
417: 
418:     check_name(fm.get("name"), dirname, errors)
419:     check_description(fm.get("description"), errors, warnings)
420: 
421:     compat = fm.get("compatibility")
422:     if compat is not None:
423:         if isinstance(compat, dict):
424:             compat = ""  # bare `compatibility:` with no value parses as an empty map
425:         if not str(compat).strip():
426:             warnings.append("compatibility is present but empty: remove it (most skills need none)")
427:         elif len(str(compat)) > MAX_COMPAT:
428:             errors.append("compatibility is %d characters (max %d)" % (len(str(compat)), MAX_COMPAT))
429: 
430:     metadata = fm.get("metadata")
431:     if metadata is not None and not isinstance(metadata, dict):
432:         warnings.append("metadata should be a map of string keys to string values, got a scalar")
433: 
434:     for key in fm:
435:         if key not in KNOWN_KEYS:
436:             warnings.append(
437:                 "unknown frontmatter key %r: only %s are defined by the spec; "
438:                 "custom properties belong under 'metadata'" % (key, ", ".join(sorted(KNOWN_KEYS)))
439:             )
440: 
441:     check_body(body, skill_dir, strict, errors, warnings)
442: 
443:     # Any bundled resource directory counts — skills legitimately organize
444:     # on-demand material under names other than references/ (e.g. rules/).
445:     RESOURCE_DIRS = ("scripts", "references", "assets", "rules", "data",
446:                      "agents", "templates", "examples")
447:     bundled = [d for d in RESOURCE_DIRS
448:                if os.path.isdir(os.path.join(skill_dir, d))]
449:     if not bundled:
450:         (errors if strict else warnings).append(
451:             "text-only skill: no bundled resources (looked for %s) — "
452:             "production-grade skills ship executable tools or on-demand "
453:             "references (the awesome-llm-apps quality bar); if all value "
454:             "lives in prose, reconsider whether this should be a skill at all"
455:             % "/, ".join(RESOURCE_DIRS)
456:         )
457: 
458:     return errors, warnings
459: 
460: 
461: def main(argv=None):
462:     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Validate an agent skill directory (agentskills.io spec).")
463:     parser.add_argument("skill_dir", help="path to the skill directory (the folder containing SKILL.md)")
464:     parser.add_argument("--strict", action="store_true", help="promote spec-limit violations (500+ line body) to errors")
465:     parser.add_argument("--json", action="store_true", dest="as_json", help="emit machine-readable JSON")
466:     args = parser.parse_args(argv)
467: 
468:     errors, warnings = lint(args.skill_dir, strict=args.strict)
469: 
470:     if args.as_json:
471:         print(json.dumps({
472:             "skill": os.path.abspath(args.skill_dir),
473:             "strict": args.strict,
474:             "errors": errors,
475:             "warnings": warnings,
476:             "passed": not errors,
477:         }, indent=2))
478:     else:
479:         print("Linting %s (strict=%s)" % (os.path.abspath(args.skill_dir), "on" if args.strict else "off"))
480:         for msg in errors:
481:             print("  ERROR: %s" % msg)
482:         for msg in warnings:
483:             print("  WARN:  %s" % msg)
484:         verdict = "PASS" if not errors else "FAIL"
485:         print("  %s — %d error(s), %d warning(s)" % (verdict, len(errors), len(warnings)))
486: 
487:     return 1 if errors else 0
488: 
489: 
490: if __name__ == "__main__":
491:     sys.exit(main())

788: def test_every_region_citation_uses_a_resolvable_form(doc_paths=DOC_PATHS):
789:     """A declared id must be cited as `<file>.md's <id>`, never bare.
790: 
791:     Added 2026-09-01, from the third whole-branch review. The dangling
792:     check above reads two forms; a citation written in any OTHER form is
793:     invisible to it, so renaming that region leaves a dead pointer no
794:     gate can see. Two shipped citations were exactly that - a backticked
795:     `brief-hash-binding` and a backticked `back-channel-auto-mirror`,
796:     the second naming no file at all.
797: 
798:     Widening the dangling regex to backticks was measured and REJECTED:
799:     over these same documents it matches eight tokens, and six are not
800:     regions at all (skill names like `gpt-5-4-prompting`, drift states
801:     like `manual-triage-needed`). This rule takes the other direction -
802:     it only ever looks at ids that ARE declared, so it cannot fire on
803:     prose - and it makes every live citation resolvable.
804: 
805:     It does NOT by itself keep the dangling check able to see a citation
806:     later; an earlier version of this docstring claimed that and was
807:     wrong. What makes that true is the `<file>.md's <id>` pattern in the
808:     dangling check, widened 2026-09-01 to read any file's possessive
809:     rather than one file's. The two rules are a pair: this one forces
810:     every citation into that spelling, and that one reads it.
811:     """
812:     import re
813: 
814:     declared_in = {}
815:     for path in doc_paths:
816:         for rid in re.findall(r"contract:start id=([a-z][a-z0-9-]*)",
817:                               path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")):
818:             declared_in.setdefault(rid, set()).add(path.name)
819:     declared = set(declared_in)
820:     assert declared, "no regions declared; this guard stopped guarding"
821:     bad = []
822:     for path in doc_paths:
823:         # Normalized: a citation that wraps across two lines is still a
824:         # citation, and reporting it as a bad form would be the reflow
825:         # trap rather than a finding.
826:         text = " ".join(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").split())
827:         for rid in declared:
828:             for match in re.finditer(re.escape(rid), text):
829:                 start, end = match.start(), match.end()
830:                 # its own marker, and any longer id that contains this one
831:                 if re.search(r"contract:start id=$", text[:start]):
832:                     continue
833:                 if start and text[start - 1] == "-":
834:                     continue
835:                 if end < len(text) and text[end] == "-":
836:                     continue
837:                 # Same file shape as the dangling check: no slash and no
838:                 # bracket, so `references/x.md's` and `(x.md's` both
839:                 # yield the bare file name the declaration is keyed by.
840:                 names = re.search(
841:                     r"([A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9._-]*\.md)'s $", text[:start])
842:                 if names and names.group(1) in declared_in.get(rid, ()):
843:                     continue
844:                 bad.append("%s cites %s" % (path.name, rid))
845:     assert not bad, (
846:         "region citation(s) not written as `<file>.md's <id>`: "
847:         f"{sorted(set(bad))}. A reader cannot resolve the region, and "
848:         "the dangling check cannot see the citation to report it dead "
849:         "when the region is renamed.")
