441: function Get-ManifestSubject($records) {
442:     # Coverage is exactly the baseline's paths. Returns @{Paths=..} or
443:     # @{Error=..}.
444:     #
445:     # Two dispositions are DEFINED and are not the same thing as skipping.
446:     # A deletion-only entry is OMITTED, deliberately, because it has no
447:     # bytes; an `RD` destination is a STOP. What must never happen is a
448:     # third thing: an entry passed over because this function could not
449:     # make sense of it. That is the silent hole in the manifest, and it is
450:     # what the stops below exist to prevent.
451:     #
452:     # Takes RECORDS, never rendered text. A rename's destination is already
453:     # the record's Path under `-z`, so there is no arrow to search for and
454:     # no chance of splitting inside a pathname.
455:     $paths = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
456:     foreach ($r in @($records)) {
457:         $x = $r.X
458:         $y = $r.Y
459:         # Deletion-only entries have no bytes to hash. HEAD plus the
460:         # baseline already bind the absence, which is the whole content of
461:         # the fact, so OMIT them.
462:         if (($x -eq " " -and $y -eq "D") -or ($x -eq "D" -and $y -eq " ")) {
463:             continue
464:         }
465:         # An `RD` destination no longer exists. That entry names no
466:         # readable file, so it is a stop rather than a silent omission.
467:         if ($y -eq "D" -and ($x -eq "R" -or $x -eq "C")) {
468:             return @{ Error = ("baseline entry '" +
469:                 (Format-StatusRecord $r) + "' names a destination that has" +
470:                 " been deleted; the mirror is not in a state this manifest" +
471:                 " rule can describe") }
472:         }
473:         [void]$paths.Add($r.Path)
474:     }
475:     return @{ Paths = @($paths) }
576: function Get-ContentManifest($repo, $paths) {
577:     # Directories expand RECURSIVELY to their files: a directory subject
578:     # such as References/ is never one manifest entry, because a hash over
579:     # a directory name identifies nothing.
580:     # A directory link beneath a subject is expanded by a listing started
581:     # at the link (Get-FilesBeneath), because a recursion from the parent
582:     # does not pass through it on either host.
583:     $files = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
584:     foreach ($p in $paths) {
585:         $full = Join-Path $repo $p
586:         if (Test-Path $full -PathType Container) {
587:             # -Stop, never the default: a swallowed enumeration error
588:             # omits every file under an unreadable subdirectory and the
589:             # manifest then reads as coverage of a tree it never saw.
590:             # Seeded with the repository root: a link back onto the root
591:             # is a cycle on its first step, not its second.
592:             $visited = New-Object "System.Collections.Generic.HashSet[string]" (
593:                 [System.StringComparer]::OrdinalIgnoreCase)
594:             [void]$visited.Add([System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($repo).TrimEnd("\"))
595:             $beneath = Get-FilesBeneath $full $visited 0
596:             if ($beneath.ContainsKey("Error")) {
597:                 return @{ Error = ("'" + $p + "': " + $beneath.Error) }
598:             }
599:             foreach ($fullName in @($beneath.Paths)) {
600:                 $rel = $fullName.Substring($repo.Length + 1)
601:                 [void]$files.Add($rel.Replace("\", "/"))
602:             }
603:         } elseif (Test-Path $full -PathType Leaf) {
604:             [void]$files.Add($p.TrimEnd("/").Replace("\", "/"))
605:         } else {
606:             # A baseline path with nothing behind it is a stop. Skipping it
607:             # would leave a hole in the manifest that reads as coverage.
608:             return @{ Error = "baseline path '$p' has no file behind it" }
609:         }
610:     }
611:     $unique = [string[]]@($files | Sort-Object -Unique)
612:     [Array]::Sort($unique, [System.StringComparer]::Ordinal)
613:     $sha = [System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::Create()
614:     $out = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
615:     foreach ($rel in $unique) {
616:         # A failed ReadAllBytes is NON-TERMINATING on both hosts
617:         # (measured 2026-08-04), so without this catch $bytes keeps the
618:         # PREVIOUS file's contents and this line records that hash for
619:         # the file it could not read - deterministically, so the wrong
620:         # value reproduces on every later run. Evidence that quietly
621:         # names the wrong bytes is worse than no evidence.
622:         $bytes = $null
623:         try {
624:             $bytes = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllBytes((Join-Path $repo $rel))
625:         } catch {
626:             $sha.Dispose()
627:             return @{ Error = ("'" + $rel + "' could not be read: " +
628:                                $_.Exception.Message) }
629:         }
630:         $hex = ([System.BitConverter]::ToString($sha.ComputeHash($bytes)) -replace '-', '').ToLower()
631:         [void]$out.Add($rel + " " + $hex)
632:     }
633:     return @{ Paths = @($out) }
634: }
635: 
636: function Get-HeadSha($repo) {
637:     # @{Ok=$true; Sha=..} or @{Ok=$false; Reason=..}. Structured for the
638:     # same reason every other capture here is: a bare empty string cannot
639:     # be told apart from a failed read, and an unread HEAD must never
640:     # compare equal to anything.
641:     $out = (& git -C $repo rev-parse HEAD 2>$null | Out-String).Trim()
642:     if (($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) -or -not $out) {
643:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = "could not resolve HEAD in $repo" }
644:     }
645:     return @{ Ok = $true; Sha = $out }
646: }
647: 
648: function Get-CombinedSha256($fields, $manifestLines) {
649:     # ONE digest over a raw status capture's fields plus a content
650:     # manifest's lines, NUL-joined - the separator git already emits
651:     # them with, so no field value can forge a boundary. Shared by the
652:     # source-side fingerprint below and the mirror-side one at
653:     # construction and verify time, so the two are computed the same way
654:     # by construction rather than by two call sites staying in sync.
655:     $joined = ((@($fields) + @($manifestLines)) -join "`0")
656:     $bytes = [System.Text.Encoding]::UTF8.GetBytes($joined)
657:     $sha = [System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::Create()
658:     try { $digest = $sha.ComputeHash($bytes) } finally { $sha.Dispose() }
659:     return (($digest | ForEach-Object { $_.ToString("x2") }) -join "")
660: }
661: 
662: function Get-StatusSha256($repo) {
663:     # A non-HEAD fingerprint for whichever repo it is given: the SAME
664:     # status capture the mirror already uses, PLUS the content manifest
665:     # of the paths that status names, combined by Get-CombinedSha256.
666:     # Used on the SOURCE for source_status_sha256 and on the MIRROR
667:     # itself for mirror_state_sha256 - one algorithm, so the two
668:     # fingerprints cannot drift apart by drifting the code that computes
669:     # them.
670:     #
671:     # THE CONTENT HALF IS NOT OPTIONAL, and measured 2026-08-04 rather
672:     # than assumed: editing an already-ignored file leaves the status
673:     # listing byte-identical, so a status-only fingerprint verified clean
674:     # across exactly the drift this check exists to catch. Status alone
675:     # sees a path APPEAR or DISAPPEAR or change code; it does not see the
676:     # bytes behind it change, and the bytes are the review input.
677:     $captured = Get-BaselineRaw $repo
678:     if (-not $captured.Ok) {
679:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = $captured.Reason }
680:     }
681:     $parsedStatus = ConvertTo-StatusRecord $captured.Fields
682:     if ($parsedStatus.ContainsKey("Error")) {
683:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = $parsedStatus.Error }
684:     }
685:     $subj = Get-ManifestSubject $parsedStatus.Records
686:     if ($subj.ContainsKey("Error")) {
687:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = $subj.Error }
688:     }
689:     $content = Get-ContentManifest $repo $subj.Paths
690:     if ($content.ContainsKey("Error")) {
691:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = $content.Error }
692:     }
693:     return @{ Ok = $true
694:               Sha = (Get-CombinedSha256 $captured.Fields $content.Paths) }
695: }

 succeeded in 194ms:
1: """Contract coverage checker.
2: 
3: Every marked region must sit WHOLE inside some pin string. There is no
4: sentence splitting: the region is the unit of coverage, and the author
5: sizes it to be pinnable by one string. Revision 1 split sentences with a
6: regex; a review produced a counterexample where two fragment pins covered
7: both halves of a mis-split sentence while nothing covered the sentence,
8: so coverage passed on an unlocked rule. Dropping the split removes that
9: bug class rather than patching it.
10: 
11: Pure functions only: no pytest, no repo paths baked in, so the fixture
12: tests can drive it over temporary directories.
13: 
14: Design: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-27-contract-coverage-design.md
15: """
16: import ast
17: import re
18: 
19: START = re.compile(r"<!--\s*contract:start\s+id=([a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*)\s*-->")
20: END = re.compile(r"<!--\s*contract:end\s*-->")
21: 
22: # A line that OPENS a comment whose keyword is contract:. Deliberately
23: # does NOT require the closing --> : an unterminated marker must be
24: # rejected, not skipped, or a typo deletes a region silently. That is the
25: # one outcome this checker may never produce.
26: #
27: # The keyword is anchored to the start of the comment on purpose.
28: # agents/implementer.md and agents/flash-implementer.md already carry
29: # <!-- shared-contract:start --> markers - the 0.12.0 parity mechanism,
30: # pinned by test_shared_contract_parity - and both files are inside the
31: # tree this checker scans. An unanchored search would call every one of
32: # them malformed and the checker could never run. Case-insensitive so a
33: # capitalized keyword is REJECTED rather than silently ignored.
34: # An OPENER of our marker family. `\s*contract\s*:` tolerates a spaced
35: # colon so that spelling is REJECTED rather than silently ignored, and
36: # case-insensitive so a capitalized keyword is rejected too. Anchored
37: # immediately after `<!--`, so `<!-- shared-contract:start -->` - the
38: # 0.12.0 parity family in agents/ - does not match and is left alone.
39: MARKERISH = re.compile(r"<!--\s*contract\s*:", re.IGNORECASE)
40: 
41: # The whole-text pass finds openers DIRECTLY rather than iterating
42: # comment spans. Iterating spans was a silent path: `re.finditer` yields
43: # NON-OVERLAPPING matches, so a stray `<!-->` earlier in the file
44: # consumes forward through a later marker's `-->`, the combined body does
45: # not start with the keyword, and the real marker is never examined by
46: # either pass. Searching for openers cannot be swallowed that way.
47: OPENER = MARKERISH
48: 
49: 
50: class MarkerError(Exception):
51:     """Malformed or mis-declared markers. Always a hard failure."""
52: 
53: 
54: def _norm(text):
55:     return " ".join(text.split())
56: 
57: 
58: def _classify(line, source, lineno):
59:     """Return ('start', id) | ('end', None) | (None, None) for one line.
60: 
61:     A marker owns its line: the whole stripped line must be the marker.
62:     That rejects an unterminated marker, a marker sharing a line with
63:     prose, and two markers on one line, all in one rule.
64:     """
65:     if not MARKERISH.search(line):
66:         return None, None
67:     text = line.strip()
68:     start = START.fullmatch(text)
69:     if start:
70:         return "start", start.group(1)
71:     if END.fullmatch(text):
72:         return "end", None
73:     raise MarkerError(
74:         f"{source}:{lineno}: malformed contract marker {text!r}. "
75:         "A marker must be alone on its line and exactly "
76:         "'<!-- contract:start id=<lowercase-id> -->' or "
77:         "'<!-- contract:end -->'.")
78: 
79: 
80: def _preflight(text, source):
81:     """Reject any marker opener that is not a clean one-line marker.
82: 
83:     Runs over the whole text, so it catches the shapes a line scan cannot
84:     see: a comment split across lines, and an unterminated one.
85: 
86:     Two details are load-bearing.
87: 
88:     The span runs from the opener to the FIRST `-->` at or after it, or
89:     to end of text when there is none, so an unterminated opener is
90:     examined rather than skipped.
91: 
92:     Single-line-ness is tested with `len(span.splitlines()) != 1`, NOT
93:     with `"\\n" in span`. `str.splitlines` also breaks on `\\r`, `\\v`,
94:     `\\f`, `\\x1c`-`\\x1e`, `\\x85`, `\\u2028` and `\\u2029`, so a marker
95:     split by a bare CR would pass a newline-only test here and then be
96:     split into two invisible halves by the line scan below - a silent
97:     deletion in a fourth shape.
98:     """
99:     for match in OPENER.finditer(text):
100:         close = text.find("-->", match.start())
101:         span = (text[match.start():close + 3] if close != -1
102:                 else text[match.start():])
103:         if (len(span.splitlines()) != 1
104:                 or not (START.fullmatch(span) or END.fullmatch(span))):
105:             raise MarkerError(
106:                 f"{source}: malformed contract marker {span[:60]!r}. "
107:                 "A marker must be a single line, exactly "
108:                 "'<!-- contract:start id=<lowercase-id> -->' or "
109:                 "'<!-- contract:end -->'.")
110: 
111: 
112: def parse_regions(text, source):
113:     """Return {region_id: normalized_body} for ONE document."""
114:     _preflight(text, source)
115:     regions = {}
116:     open_id = None
117:     buf = []
118:     for lineno, line in enumerate(text.splitlines(), 1):
119:         kind, rid = _classify(line, source, lineno)
120:         if kind == "start":
121:             if open_id is not None:
122:                 raise MarkerError(
123:                     f"{source}:{lineno}: region '{rid}' opens inside "
124:                     f"still-open region '{open_id}'")
125:             open_id = rid
126:             buf = []
127:             continue
128:         if kind == "end":
129:             if open_id is None:
130:                 raise MarkerError(f"{source}:{lineno}: end with no start")
131:             body = _norm(" ".join(buf))
132:             if not body:
133:                 raise MarkerError(
134:                     f"{source}:{lineno}: region '{open_id}' is empty")
135:             if open_id in regions:
136:                 raise MarkerError(
137:                     f"{source}:{lineno}: duplicate id '{open_id}'")
138:             regions[open_id] = body
139:             open_id = None
140:             continue
141:         if open_id is not None:
142:             buf.append(line)
143:     if open_id is not None:
144:         raise MarkerError(f"{source}: region '{open_id}' never closed")
145:     return regions
146: 
147: 
148: def collect_regions(paths):
149:     """Return {region_id: (body, source_name)} across many documents.
150: 
151:     Ids are unique repo-wide, not per file: one subject stated in two
152:     documents needs two ids, or a failure message cannot say which file
153:     to open.
154:     """
155:     out = {}
156:     for path in paths:
157:         found = parse_regions(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"), path.name)
158:         for rid, body in found.items():
159:             if rid in out:
160:                 raise MarkerError(
161:                     f"duplicate region id '{rid}' in {out[rid][1]} "
162:                     f"and {path.name}")
163:             out[rid] = (body, path.name)
164:     return out
165: 
166: 
167: def _literal(node):
168:     """The node's own string value, or nothing.
169: 
170:     Deliberately NOT a walk. Walking every constant below an operand
171:     collects both branches of a conditional: `assert ("x" if flag else
172:     "y") in body` requires only the selected value, yet a walk returns
173:     both, so the unselected one becomes a pin the assertion never checks.
174: 
175:     Adjacent string literals are already folded into ONE constant by the
176:     parser, which is how nearly every pin in this repo is written.
177: 
178:     The cost is real but bounded, and worth stating exactly rather than
179:     waving away: five fragments are lost, all from runtime-constructed
180:     needles such as `"--model " + CANONICAL_ID` and
181:     `'model="' + CANONICAL_ID + '"'`. Those assertions DO require their
182:     fragments to be present, so these are genuine partial locks, not
183:     noise. They are dropped deliberately. The correct claim is that the
184:     strict rule costs no CURRENT MARKED COVERAGE - all nine regions in
185:     scope and all three history controls are unaffected - not that it
186:     costs nothing.
187:     """
188:     if isinstance(node, ast.Constant) and isinstance(node.value, str):
189:         return {_norm(node.value)}
190:     return set()
191: 
192: 
193: def _is_count_call(node):
194:     """Any `<something>.count(...)` call.
195: 
196:     Accepted limit: ast sees a method NAME, never a type, so a
197:     `list.count(...)` would register too. Every live receiver is a
198:     document string, and checking the type is not possible from the
199:     syntax tree, so the limit is stated rather than closed.
200:     """
201:     return (isinstance(node, ast.Call)
202:             and isinstance(node.func, ast.Attribute)
203:             and node.func.attr == "count")
204: 
205: 
206: def _clause_pins(node):
207:     """Pins from a COMPLETE positive-presence clause, or nothing.
208: 
209:     This function NEVER descends into an expression it does not
210:     recognize, and that restraint is the whole point. Matching a shape
211:     anywhere in the tree lets an enclosing expression flip its meaning:
212:     `assert ("lit" in body) == False` and `assert flag or "lit" in body`
213:     both CONTAIN a positive membership test that the assertion as a whole
214:     does not require. The second is live, at
215:     evals/multi-model-verify/test_flash_implementer.py:58, where the
216:     assertion permits either the absence of "stdin" or the presence of
217:     "does not reach" - so treating the latter as an unconditional pin
218:     would manufacture coverage.
219: 
220:     Three clause forms and no others:
221:       "literal" in body
222:       body.count("literal"), alone or compared == n / >= n (n >= 1)
223:                              or > n (n >= 0)
224:       an `and`, which contributes each operand it recognizes
225: 
226:     The conjunction rule is stated that way on purpose. A MIXED `and`
227:     such as `assert "lit" in body and flag` contributes "lit" and drops
228:     the rest, rather than being rejected whole. That is sound: the
229:     assertion passes only if every operand is true, so a recognized
230:     operand's requirement holds regardless of what sits beside it.
231:     Rejecting mixed conjunctions would discard real locks for no gain.
232: 
233:     Measured on the live suite: an unrestricted walk of `ast.Assert`
234:     yields 715 strings, of which 192 are only reachable through a failure
235:     message and lock nothing, and 19 sit in `not in` comparisons that
236:     assert ABSENCE. The clause rule yields 366, and every region in scope
237:     keeps the coverage it had.
238: 
239:     Never called on `Assert.msg`.
240:     """
241:     if isinstance(node, ast.BoolOp) and isinstance(node.op, ast.And):
242:         pins = set()
243:         for value in node.values:
244:             pins |= _clause_pins(value)
245:         return pins
246:     if isinstance(node, ast.Compare) and len(node.ops) == 1:
247:         op, right = node.ops[0], node.comparators[0]
248:         if isinstance(op, ast.In):
249:             return _literal(node.left)
250:         if (_is_count_call(node.left)
251:                 and len(node.left.args) == 1
252:                 and not node.left.keywords
253:                 and isinstance(right, ast.Constant)
254:                 and isinstance(right.value, int)
255:                 and not isinstance(right.value, bool)):
256:             n = right.value
257:             positive = ((isinstance(op, (ast.Eq, ast.GtE)) and n >= 1)
258:                         or (isinstance(op, ast.Gt) and n >= 0))
259:             if positive:
260:                 return _literal(node.left.args[0])
261:         return set()
262:     if _is_count_call(node) and len(node.args) == 1 and not node.keywords:
263:         return _literal(node.args[0])
264:     return set()
265: 
266: 
267: # Context managers that swallow the exception an assertion raises.
268: # Matched by the called NAME, so `pytest.raises(...)`, a bare
269: # `raises(...)` from `from pytest import raises`, and the contextlib
270: # equivalents are all caught.
271: CONSUMING_CALLS = frozenset({"raises", "suppress"})
272: 
273: 
274: def _is_consuming_with(node):
275:     """True for a `with` block whose body's failures are swallowed."""
276:     if not isinstance(node, (ast.With, ast.AsyncWith)):
277:         return False
278:     for item in node.items:
279:         call = item.context_expr
280:         if not isinstance(call, ast.Call):
281:             continue
282:         func = call.func
283:         if isinstance(func, ast.Attribute) and func.attr in CONSUMING_CALLS:
284:             return True
285:         if isinstance(func, ast.Name) and func.id in CONSUMING_CALLS:
286:             return True
287:     return False
288: 
289: 
290: def _is_xfail_decorated(node):
291:     """True for a function marked as expected to fail."""
292:     if not isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):
293:         return False
294:     for decorator in node.decorator_list:
295:         expr = decorator.func if isinstance(decorator, ast.Call) else decorator
296:         while isinstance(expr, ast.Attribute):
297:             if expr.attr == "xfail":
298:                 return True
299:             expr = expr.value
300:         if isinstance(expr, ast.Name) and expr.id == "xfail":
301:             return True
302:     return False
303: 
304: 
305: def _assert_tests(node, consumed=False):
306:     """Yield the test of every assertion whose failure reaches the runner.
307: 
308:     An assertion locks its text only because failing it fails the suite.
309:     Where that failure is deliberately caught, the assertion proves the
310:     OPPOSITE and must contribute nothing.
311: 
312:     The shape that forced this, found by the cross-vendor lane in the
313:     0.15.0 diff debate:
314: 
315:         with pytest.raises(AssertionError):
316:             assert "Entire marked region." in body
317: 
318:     That test passes when the region text is ABSENT, yet an unrestricted
319:     walk registered the literal and the region read as covered - false
320:     coverage, the one direction this checker may never produce. It is not
321:     the execution-blind limit the design already accepts: the assertion
322:     runs, and its failure is eaten.
323: 
324:     Detection is by ENCLOSING CONTEXT, not by assertion shape, because
325:     the assertion itself is identical either way.
326: 
327:     Deliberately conservative on three counts. Any `raises(...)` counts,
328:     not only `raises(AssertionError)` - narrowing it would mean tracking
329:     which exception types an assert can raise. Every statement in the
330:     body of a `try` that HAS HANDLERS counts, whatever those handlers
331:     catch. And an xfail marker disqualifies the whole function. Each
332:     over-rejection loses a pin,
333:     which reads UNCOVERED, which is a red. Under-rejecting manufactures
334:     coverage. Only one of those is safe to get wrong.
335: 
336:     Costs nothing today: no live pin file contains any of these shapes.
337:     """
338:     if isinstance(node, ast.Assert):
339:         if not consumed:
340:             yield node.test
341:         return
342:     if _is_consuming_with(node) or _is_xfail_decorated(node):
343:         consumed = True
344:     if isinstance(node, (ast.Try, ast.TryStar)):
345:         # Only a handler can catch the failure. `try/finally` runs the
346:         # cleanup and then lets the AssertionError through, so its body
347:         # is ordinary asserting code. Consuming it too was stricter than
348:         # this module's own documented rule and cost real locks for no
349:         # safety - found in the round-2 fix re-review of this very fix.
350:         body_consumed = consumed or bool(node.handlers)
351:         for child in node.body:
352:             yield from _assert_tests(child, body_consumed)
353:         for group in (node.handlers, node.orelse, node.finalbody):
354:             for child in group:
355:                 yield from _assert_tests(child, consumed)
356:         return
357:     for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(node):
358:         yield from _assert_tests(child, consumed)
359: 
360: 
361: def collect_pins(paths):
362:     """Normalized string literals that some assertion checks for.
363: 
364:     Read through ast, not regex: nearly every pin in this repo is written
365:     as adjacent string literals across several lines, and the parser
366:     joins those into one constant for us.
367: 
368:     Only assertions whose failure reaches the runner are read; see
369:     `_assert_tests` for why, and for the shape that proved it necessary.
370: 
371:     Accepted limits, all with the same safe failure direction - the
372:     region reads UNCOVERED, which is a red, never false coverage: a
373:     string bound to a name and asserted through that name, a regex lock
374:     such as `re.search(r"...", text)`, and a literal compared with `==`.
375: 
376:     Some limits run the OTHER way and could in principle manufacture
377:     coverage. The design tags every limit by direction and states NO
378:     total, because the total was written as "one", corrected to "two",
379:     and was still wrong. Read the tags there rather than trusting a count
380:     here.
381:     """
382:     pins = set()
383:     for path in paths:
384:         tree = ast.parse(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
385:         for test in _assert_tests(tree):
386:             pins |= _clause_pins(test)
387:     return pins
388: 
389: 
390: def uncovered(regions, pins):
391:     """[(region_id, source, body)] for every region no pin contains.
392: 
393:     Containment runs one way only: the PIN must contain the REGION. A pin
394:     that the region contains is a fragment, which is exactly the defect
395:     this checker exists to catch.
396:     """
397:     misses = []
398:     for rid in sorted(regions):
399:         body, source = regions[rid]
400:         if not any(body in pin for pin in pins):
401:             misses.append((rid, source, body))
402:     return misses
403: 
404: 
405: def format_failure(misses):
406:     lines = [
407:         f"{len(misses)} contract region(s) are not locked by any pin.",
408:         "For each one, add a pin containing that region whole.",
409:         "A pin the region contains is a fragment and does not count.",
410:         "",
411:     ]
412:     for rid, source, body in misses:
413:         lines.append(f"  region '{rid}' in {source}:")
414:         lines.append(f"    {body}")
415:     return "\n".join(lines)
